442	def _write_json(path: Path, payload: Any) -> None:
443	    Path(path).write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
444	
445	def _raise_unknown_keys(path: str, payload: dict[str, Any], allowed: frozenset[str]) -> None:
446	    for key in payload:
447	        if key not in allowed:
448	            raise ValueError(f"{path} has unknown key {key!r}")
449	
450	def _normalize_clip_for_validation(clip: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
451	    normalized = dict(clip)
452	    if "from_" in normalized and "from" not in normalized:
453	        normalized["from"] = normalized.pop("from_")
454	    return normalized
455	
456	def _known_timeline_payload(config: Mapping[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
457	    return {key: value for key, value in config.items() if key in _TIMELINE_TOP_ALLOWED}

480	def _animation_meta(animation_id: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
481	    try:
482	        from artagents.core.element import catalog as effects_catalog
483	    except ImportError:
484	        from artagents.core.element import catalog as effects_catalog  # type: ignore[no-redef]
485	    try:
486	        return effects_catalog.read_animation_meta(animation_id)
487	    except Exception:
488	        return {}
489	
490	def _validate_animation_reference(ref: Any, phase: str, path: str, known_ids: set[str]) -> None:
491	    if isinstance(ref, str):
492	        animation_id = ref
493	    elif isinstance(ref, dict):
494	        _raise_unknown_keys(path, ref, frozenset({"id", "durationFrames", "easing", "params"}))
495	        animation_id = ref.get("id")
496	        if "durationFrames" in ref and (
497	            not isinstance(ref.get("durationFrames"), (int, float)) or float(ref["durationFrames"]) <= 0
498	        ):
499	            raise ValueError(f"{path}.durationFrames must be a positive number")
500	        if "easing" in ref and not isinstance(ref.get("easing"), str):
501	            raise ValueError(f"{path}.easing must be a string")
502	        if "params" in ref and not isinstance(ref.get("params"), dict):
503	            raise ValueError(f"{path}.params must be an object")
504	    else:
505	        raise ValueError(f"{path} must be an animation id string or object")
506	    if not isinstance(animation_id, str) or not animation_id:
507	        raise ValueError(f"{path}.id must be a non-empty string")
508	    if known_ids and animation_id not in known_ids:
509	        raise ValueError(f"{path} animation id {animation_id!r} is not present in the animations catalog")
510	    meta = _animation_meta(animation_id)
511	    meta_phase = meta.get("phase")
512	    phase_matches = (
513	        meta_phase in (None, "any", phase)
514	        or (isinstance(meta_phase, list) and phase in meta_phase)
515	    )
516	    if not phase_matches:
517	        raise ValueError(f"{path} animation {animation_id!r} has phase {meta_phase!r}, expected {phase!r}")
518	
519	def _validate_animation_reference_list(value: Any, phase: str, path: str, known_ids: set[str]) -> None:
520	    if value is None:
521	        return
522	    refs = value if isinstance(value, list) else [value]
523	    if not refs:
524	        raise ValueError(f"{path} must not be an empty animation list")
525	    for index, ref in enumerate(refs):
526	        _validate_animation_reference(ref, phase, f"{path}[{index}]", known_ids)
527	
528	def _schema_params_for_animation_refs(schema: dict[str, Any], params: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
529	    """Keep legacy strict effect schemas usable while standardized animation refs roll out."""
530	    properties = schema.get("properties")
531	    if not isinstance(properties, dict):
532	        return params
533	    next_params = dict(params)
534	    for phase in ("entrance", "sustain", "exit"):
535	        if phase not in next_params:
536	            continue
537	        prop = properties.get(phase)
538	        if not isinstance(prop, dict):
539	            next_params.pop(phase)
540	            continue
541	        enum = prop.get("enum")
542	        if isinstance(enum, list) and "none" in enum:
543	            next_params[phase] = "none"
544	        elif prop.get("type") == "string":
545	            next_params[phase] = "none"
546	    return next_params
547	
548	def _validate_effect_params(effect_id: str, params: Any, path: str, theme: str | None = None) -> None:
549	    if params is None:
550	        return
551	    if not isinstance(params, dict):
552	        raise ValueError(f"{path} must be an object")
553	    known_animation_ids = _animation_ids()
554	    for phase in ("entrance", "sustain", "exit"):
555	        if phase in params:
556	            _validate_animation_reference_list(params[phase], phase, f"{path}.{phase}", known_animation_ids)
557	    try:
558	        from artagents.core.element import catalog as effects_catalog
559	        import jsonschema  # type: ignore[import-not-found]
560	    except ImportError:
561	        try:
562	            from artagents.core.element import catalog as effects_catalog  # type: ignore[no-redef]
563	            import jsonschema  # type: ignore[import-not-found,no-redef]
564	        except ImportError:
565	            return
566	    schema = effects_catalog.read_effect_schema(effect_id, theme=theme)
567	    jsonschema.validate(_schema_params_for_animation_refs(schema, params), schema)
568	
569	def _transition_reference(value: Any, path: str) -> tuple[str, float | None]:
570	    if isinstance(value, str):
571	        return value, None
572	    if not isinstance(value, dict):
573	        raise ValueError(f"{path} must be a transition id string or object")
574	    _raise_unknown_keys(path, value, frozenset({"id", "type", "duration", "durationFrames", "params"}))
575	    transition_id = value.get("id", value.get("type"))
576	    if not isinstance(transition_id, str) or not transition_id:
577	        raise ValueError(f"{path}.id must be a non-empty string")
578	    if "params" in value and not isinstance(value.get("params"), dict):
579	        raise ValueError(f"{path}.params must be an object")
580	    duration_frames = value.get("durationFrames")
581	    duration_seconds = value.get("duration")
582	    if duration_frames is not None:
583	        if not isinstance(duration_frames, (int, float)) or float(duration_frames) <= 0:
584	            raise ValueError(f"{path}.durationFrames must be a positive number")
585	        return transition_id, float(duration_frames)
586	    if duration_seconds is not None:
587	        if not isinstance(duration_seconds, (int, float)) or float(duration_seconds) <= 0:
588	            raise ValueError(f"{path}.duration must be a positive number")
589	        return transition_id, None
590	    return transition_id, None
591	
592	def _clip_duration_seconds(clip: dict[str, Any]) -> float | None:
593	    hold = clip.get("hold")
594	    if isinstance(hold, (int, float)) and float(hold) >= 0:
595	        return float(hold)
596	    start = clip.get("from", 0)
597	    end = clip.get("to")
598	    if isinstance(start, (int, float)) and isinstance(end, (int, float)) and float(end) >= float(start):
599	        return float(end) - float(start)
600	    return None
601	
602	def _validate_clip_transitions(clips: list[dict[str, Any]], fps: float) -> None:
603	    known_ids = _transition_ids()
604	    by_track: dict[str, list[tuple[int, dict[str, Any]]]] = {}
605	    for index, clip in enumerate(clips):
606	        track = clip.get("track")
607	        if isinstance(track, str):
608	            by_track.setdefault(track, []).append((index, clip))
609	    for track_clips in by_track.values():
610	        track_clips.sort(key=lambda item: float(item[1].get("at", 0)))
611	        for position, (index, clip) in enumerate(track_clips):
612	            if "transition" not in clip:
613	                continue
614	            transition_id, duration_frames = _transition_reference(clip["transition"], f"clips[{index}].transition")
615	            if known_ids and transition_id not in known_ids:
616	                raise ValueError(
617	                    f"clips[{index}].transition id {transition_id!r} is not present in the transitions catalog"
618	                )
619	            next_clip = track_clips[position + 1][1] if position + 1 < len(track_clips) else None
620	            current_duration = _clip_duration_seconds(clip)
621	            next_duration = _clip_duration_seconds(next_clip) if next_clip is not None else None
622	            duration_seconds = duration_frames / fps if duration_frames is not None else None
623	            if duration_seconds is None and isinstance(clip.get("transition"), dict):
624	                raw_duration = clip["transition"].get("duration")
625	                duration_seconds = float(raw_duration) if isinstance(raw_duration, (int, float)) else None
626	            if duration_seconds is None or current_duration is None or next_duration is None:
627	                continue
628	            if duration_seconds > current_duration or duration_seconds > next_duration:
629	                raise ValueError(
630	                    f"clips[{index}].transition duration {duration_seconds:.3f}s must fit both adjacent same-track clips"
631	                )
632	
633	def _timeline_fps(config: dict[str, Any]) -> float:
634	    """Best-effort fps for timeline-internal validation (transitions, etc.).
635	
636	    The authoritative fps comes from theme.visual.canvas at render time. This helper
637	    looks for a theme_overrides override; otherwise it returns a sentinel default
638	    (30) used only for clip-transition duration checks.
639	    """
640	    overrides = config.get("theme_overrides")
641	    if isinstance(overrides, dict):
642	        visual = overrides.get("visual")
643	        if isinstance(visual, dict):
644	            canvas = visual.get("canvas")
645	            if isinstance(canvas, dict):
646	                fps_value = canvas.get("fps")
647	                if isinstance(fps_value, (int, float)) and float(fps_value) > 0:
648	                    return float(fps_value)
649	    return 30.0
650	
651	
652	def _swap_from_load(clip: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
653	    if "from" in clip:
654	        clip["from_"] = clip.pop("from")
655	    return clip
656	
657	def _swap_from_dump(clip: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
658	    out = dict(clip)
659	    if "from_" in out:
660	        out["from"] = out.pop("from_")
661	    return out
662	
663	def _round_at_for_dump(clip: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
664	    if "at" in clip and isinstance(clip["at"], (int, float)):
665	        clip["at"] = round(float(clip["at"]), 3)
666	    return clip
667	
668	
669	def _asset_entry_for_clip(
670	    clip: Mapping[str, Any],
671	    registry: AssetRegistry | Mapping[str, Any] | None,
672	) -> Mapping[str, Any] | None:
673	    asset_key = clip.get("asset")
674	    if not isinstance(asset_key, str) or not isinstance(registry, Mapping):
675	        return None
676	    assets = registry.get("assets")
677	    if not isinstance(assets, Mapping):
678	        return None
679	    entry = assets.get(asset_key)
680	    return entry if isinstance(entry, Mapping) else None
681	
682	
683	def _asset_kind_from_entry(entry: Mapping[str, Any] | None) -> ClipClassifiedKind | None:
684	    if not isinstance(entry, Mapping):
685	        return None
686	    raw_type = entry.get("type")
687	    if isinstance(raw_type, str):
688	        normalized = raw_type.lower()
689	        if normalized in {"video", "image", "audio"}:
690	            return ClipClassifiedKind(normalized)
691	        if normalized.startswith("video/"):
692	            return ClipClassifiedKind.VIDEO
693	        if normalized.startswith("image/"):
694	            return ClipClassifiedKind.IMAGE
695	        if normalized.startswith("audio/"):
696	            return ClipClassifiedKind.AUDIO
697	    for key in ("mime", "mimeType", "contentType"):
698	        mime = entry.get(key)
699	        if not isinstance(mime, str):
700	            continue
701	        normalized = mime.lower()
702	        if normalized.startswith("video/"):
703	            return ClipClassifiedKind.VIDEO
704	        if normalized.startswith("image/"):
705	            return ClipClassifiedKind.IMAGE
706	        if normalized.startswith("audio/"):
707	            return ClipClassifiedKind.AUDIO
708	    return None
709	
710	
711	def _classify_clip(
712	    clip: Mapping[str, Any],
713	    registry: AssetRegistry | Mapping[str, Any] | None = None,
714	    *,
715	    theme: str | None = None,
716	) -> ClipClassifiedKind:
717	    clip_type = clip.get("clipType", "media")
718	    if clip_type == "text":
719	        return ClipClassifiedKind.TEXT
720	    if clip_type == "media":
721	        return _asset_kind_from_entry(_asset_entry_for_clip(clip, registry)) or ClipClassifiedKind.OPAQUE
722	    if clip_type in {"hold"}:
723	        return _asset_kind_from_entry(_asset_entry_for_clip(clip, registry)) or ClipClassifiedKind.VIDEO
724	    if clip_type == "effect-layer":
725	        return ClipClassifiedKind.EFFECT
726	    if isinstance(clip_type, str) and clip_type in _effect_ids(theme):
727	        return ClipClassifiedKind.EFFECT
728	    return ClipClassifiedKind.OPAQUE

765	class Timeline:
766	    """Persisted timeline JSON with top-level passthrough fields preserved."""

768	    def __init__(self, config: Mapping[str, Any], *, validate: bool = True) -> None:

770	        if validate:
771	            validate_timeline(data)
772	        self._data = data

774	    @classmethod
775	    def from_json_data(cls, payload: Mapping[str, Any], *, validate: bool = True) -> "Timeline":
776	        data = copy.deepcopy(dict(payload))
777	        clips = data.get("clips")
778	        if isinstance(clips, list):
779	            data["clips"] = [
780	                _swap_from_load(dict(clip)) if isinstance(clip, dict) else clip
781	                for clip in clips

790	        return cls.from_json_data(data, validate=validate)

824	    @property

827	        return value if isinstance(value, dict) else None  # type: ignore[return-value]
828	
829	    def classified_clips(
830	        self,
831	        registry: AssetRegistry | Mapping[str, Any] | None = None,
832	    ) -> list[TimelineClipView]:
833	        return [
834	            TimelineClipView(clip, registry=registry, theme=self.theme)
835	            for clip in self.clips
836	            if isinstance(clip, Mapping)

839	    def for_render(self, default_theme: str = "banodoco-default") -> TimelineRenderView:
840	        if not isinstance(default_theme, str) or not default_theme:
841	            raise ValueError("default_theme must be a non-empty slug")
842	        return TimelineRenderView(self, default_theme=default_theme)

844	    def to_config(self) -> TimelineConfig:
845	        return copy.deepcopy(self._data)  # type: ignore[return-value]
846	
847	    def to_json_data(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
848	        payload = copy.deepcopy(self._data)
849	        clips = payload.get("clips")
850	        if isinstance(clips, list):
851	            payload["clips"] = [
852	                _round_at_for_dump(_swap_from_dump(dict(clip))) if isinstance(clip, dict) else clip
853	                for clip in clips
854	            ]
855	        validate_timeline(payload)
856	        return payload

861	def merge_generation(
862	    theme_generation: dict[str, Any] | None,
863	    per_clip: dict[str, Any] | None,
864	) -> dict[str, Any]:
865	    """Merge per-clip generation atop the resolved theme.generation block.
866	
867	    Per-clip keys win on conflict. Lists (references, assets) are replaced wholesale,
868	    not merged. Returns an empty dict if both inputs are empty/None.
869	    """
870	    merged: dict[str, Any] = {}
871	    if isinstance(theme_generation, dict):
872	        merged.update(theme_generation)
873	    if isinstance(per_clip, dict):
874	        merged.update(per_clip)
875	    return merged
876	
877	
878	def _deep_merge_theme(base: dict[str, Any], overlay: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
879	    """Merge overlay onto base for theme blocks.
880	
881	    Top-level theme keys (visual, generation, voice, audio, pacing) are merged at one
882	    level deep. Nested dicts inside (e.g. visual.canvas) are merged key-by-key. Lists
883	    such as generation.references and generation.assets are replaced wholesale.
884	    """
885	    result: dict[str, Any] = {key: value for key, value in base.items()}
886	    for key, value in overlay.items():
887	        if (
888	            key in result
889	            and isinstance(result[key], dict)
890	            and isinstance(value, dict)
891	        ):
892	            merged_block: dict[str, Any] = dict(result[key])
893	            for sub_key, sub_value in value.items():
894	                if (
895	                    sub_key in merged_block
896	                    and isinstance(merged_block[sub_key], dict)
897	                    and isinstance(sub_value, dict)
898	                ):
899	                    inner = dict(merged_block[sub_key])
900	                    inner.update(sub_value)
901	                    merged_block[sub_key] = inner
902	                else:
903	                    merged_block[sub_key] = sub_value
904	            result[key] = merged_block
905	        else:
906	            result[key] = value
907	    return result
908	
909	
910	def resolve_timeline_theme(timeline: "TimelineConfig", themes_root: Path) -> dict[str, Any]:
911	    """Return the merged theme view: theme.json + timeline.theme_overrides.
912	
913	    `timeline['theme']` is a slug resolved against `<themes_root>/<slug>/theme.json`.
914	    Overrides are deep-merged onto the loaded theme; list-valued fields (references,
915	    assets) are replaced wholesale by the override.
916	    """
917	    slug = timeline.get("theme") if isinstance(timeline, dict) else None
918	    if not isinstance(slug, str) or not slug:
919	        raise ValueError("Timeline.theme must be a non-empty slug")
920	    theme_path = Path(themes_root) / slug / "theme.json"
921	    if not theme_path.is_file():
922	        raise FileNotFoundError(f"Theme {slug!r} not found at {theme_path}")
923	    base = json.loads(theme_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
924	    if not isinstance(base, dict):
925	        raise ValueError(f"Theme file {theme_path} must contain a JSON object")
926	    overrides = timeline.get("theme_overrides") if isinstance(timeline, dict) else None
927	    if isinstance(overrides, dict) and overrides:
928	        return _deep_merge_theme(base, overrides)
929	    return base

964	def _assign_missing_arrangement_uuids(arrangement: Any) -> bool:
965	    if not isinstance(arrangement, dict):
966	        return False
967	    clips = arrangement.get("clips")
968	    if not isinstance(clips, list):
969	        return False
970	    used = {
971	        clip.get("uuid")
972	        for clip in clips
973	        if isinstance(clip, dict) and isinstance(clip.get("uuid"), str)
974	    }
975	    assigned = False
976	    for clip in clips:
977	        if not isinstance(clip, dict) or "uuid" in clip:
978	            continue
979	        value = uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]
980	        while value in used:
981	            value = uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]
982	        used.add(value)
983	        clip["uuid"] = value
984	        assigned = True
985	        order = clip.get("order")
986	        print(f"timeline.load_arrangement: migrated clip order={order} uuid={value}", file=sys.stderr)
987	    return assigned
988	
989	def load_arrangement(
990	    path: Path,
991	    pool_ids: set[str] | None = None,
992	    *,
993	    assign_missing_uuids: bool = False,
994	) -> Arrangement:
995	    data = json.loads(Path(path).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
996	    migrated = False
997	    if assign_missing_uuids:
998	        migrated = _assign_missing_arrangement_uuids(data)
999	    validate_arrangement(data, pool_ids)
1000	    if migrated:
1001	        _write_json(Path(path), data)
1002	    return data  # type: ignore[return-value]
1003	
1004	def save_arrangement(arrangement: Arrangement, path: Path, pool_ids: set[str] | None = None) -> None:
1005	    validate_arrangement(arrangement, pool_ids)
1006	    _write_json(path, arrangement)

1008	def validate_registry(registry: Any) -> None:
1009	    if not isinstance(registry, dict):
1010	        raise ValueError("Asset registry must be a JSON object")
1011	    _raise_unknown_keys("Asset registry", registry, frozenset({"assets"}))
1012	    assets = registry.get("assets")
1013	    if not isinstance(assets, dict):
1014	        raise ValueError("Asset registry.assets must be an object")
1015	    for key, entry in assets.items():
1016	        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
1017	            raise ValueError(f"Asset registry.assets[{key!r}] must be an object")
1018	        _raise_unknown_keys(f"Asset registry.assets[{key!r}]", entry, _ASSET_ENTRY_ALLOWED)
1019	        if "file" not in entry and "url" not in entry:
1020	            raise ValueError(f"Asset {key!r} must have 'file' or 'url'")
1021	        url = entry.get("url")
1022	        if url is not None and (not isinstance(url, str) or not url.startswith(("http://", "https://"))):
1023	            raise ValueError(f"Asset {key!r}.url must be an http(s) URL")
1024	        content_sha256 = entry.get("content_sha256")
1025	        if content_sha256 is not None and (
1026	            not isinstance(content_sha256, str) or re.fullmatch(r"[0-9a-f]{64}", content_sha256) is None
1027	        ):
1028	            raise ValueError(f"Asset {key!r}.content_sha256 must be a 64-character lowercase hex string")
1029	        if "url_expires_at" in entry:
1030	            _validate_generated_at(entry.get("url_expires_at"), f"Asset {key!r}.url_expires_at")
1031	        etag = entry.get("etag")
1032	        if etag is not None and (not isinstance(etag, str) or not etag):
1033	            raise ValueError(f"Asset {key!r}.etag must be a non-empty string")
1034	
1035	def validate_timeline(config: Any, *, strict: bool = True) -> None:
1036	    """Validate a Banodoco timeline.
1037	
1038	    `clipType` is an open string for Reigh compatibility. Built-in media/text
1039	    clips and registered effects get semantic checks where ArtAgents knows how;
1040	    unknown clip types stay valid and classify as opaque at runtime.
1041	
1042	    Callers that need to accept legacy/under-construction timelines (e.g.
1043	    in-flight pipeline outputs that reference theme content not yet on
1044	    disk) can opt into `strict=False`.
1045	    """
1046	    if not isinstance(config, dict):
1047	        raise ValueError("Timeline must be a JSON object")
1048	    theme = config.get("theme")
1049	    if theme is not None and (not isinstance(theme, str) or not theme):
1050	        raise ValueError("Timeline.theme must be a non-empty slug")
1051	    # Shape-check against the shared JSON Schema first; then run the
1052	    # Banodoco-only semantic checks (effect-id registry, transition durations).
1053	    normalized_for_shared = _known_timeline_payload(config)
1054	    if isinstance(normalized_for_shared.get("clips"), list):
1055	        normalized_for_shared["clips"] = [
1056	            _normalize_clip_for_validation(c) if isinstance(c, dict) else c
1057	            for c in normalized_for_shared["clips"]
1058	        ]
1059	    _shared_validate_timeline(normalized_for_shared, strict=strict)
1060	    fps = _timeline_fps(config)
1061	    tracks = config.get("tracks")
1062	    if tracks is not None:
1063	        if not isinstance(tracks, list):
1064	            raise ValueError("Timeline.tracks must be a list")
1065	        for index, track in enumerate(tracks):
1066	            if not isinstance(track, dict):
1067	                raise ValueError(f"tracks[{index}] must be an object")
1068	            _raise_unknown_keys(f"tracks[{index}]", track, _TRACK_ALLOWED)
1069	            for field in ("id", "kind", "label"):
1070	                if field not in track:
1071	                    raise ValueError(f"tracks[{index}].{field} is required")
1072	    overrides = config.get("theme_overrides")
1073	    if overrides is not None:
1074	        if not isinstance(overrides, dict):
1075	            raise ValueError("Timeline.theme_overrides must be an object")
1076	        _raise_unknown_keys("Timeline.theme_overrides", overrides, _THEME_OVERRIDES_ALLOWED)
1077	    clips = config.get("clips")
1078	    if not isinstance(clips, list):
1079	        raise ValueError("Timeline.clips must be a list")
1080	    clip_ids: set[str] = set()
1081	    normalized_clips: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
1082	    for index, clip_raw in enumerate(clips):
1083	        if not isinstance(clip_raw, dict):
1084	            raise ValueError(f"clips[{index}] must be an object")
1085	        clip = _normalize_clip_for_validation(clip_raw)
1086	        normalized_clips.append(clip)
1087	        _raise_unknown_keys(f"clips[{index}]", clip, _CLIP_ALLOWED)
1088	        for field in ("id", "at", "track"):
1089	            if field not in clip:
1090	                raise ValueError(f"clips[{index}].{field} is required")
1091	        clip_type = clip.get("clipType", "media")
1092	        if not isinstance(clip_type, str):
1093	            raise ValueError(f"clips[{index}].clipType must be a string")
1094	        # Active theme slug from the timeline lets effect-id scans pick up
1095	        # theme-scoped clipTypes (e.g. 2rp's section-hook). Open-string
1096	        # clipTypes that are not registered effects remain opaque.
1097	        active_theme = theme if isinstance(theme, str) else None
1098	        effect_ids = _effect_ids(active_theme)
1099	        if clip_type in effect_ids:
1100	            _validate_effect_params(clip_type, clip.get("params"), f"clips[{index}].params", theme=active_theme)
1101	        if "pool_id" in clip and not isinstance(clip["pool_id"], str):
1102	            raise ValueError(f"clips[{index}].pool_id must be a string")
1103	        if "clip_order" in clip:
1104	            order = clip["clip_order"]
1105	            if not isinstance(order, int) or isinstance(order, bool) or order <= 0:
1106	                raise ValueError(f"clips[{index}].clip_order must be a positive integer")
1107	        if clip["id"] in clip_ids:
1108	            raise ValueError(f"clips[{index}].id {clip['id']!r} is not unique")
1109	        clip_ids.add(clip["id"])
1110	    _validate_clip_transitions(normalized_clips, float(fps))
1111	
1112	def validate_metadata(meta: Any) -> None:
1113	    if not isinstance(meta, dict):
1114	        raise ValueError("Metadata must be a JSON object")
1115	    if meta.get("version") != METADATA_VERSION:
1116	        raise ValueError(f"Metadata.version must be {METADATA_VERSION}")
1117	    generated_at = meta.get("generated_at")
1118	    if not isinstance(generated_at, str) or not generated_at or not generated_at.endswith("Z"):
1119	        raise ValueError("Metadata.generated_at must be a non-empty UTC timestamp ending in 'Z'")

Perfect! Now I have enough information. Let me create a comprehensive summary:

## Summary

Based on my exploration of the ArtAgents codebase, here's how projects and timelines relate:

### 1. **Creating a Project (No Explicit "Project New" CLI)**
There is **no `artagents project new` command**. Instead, projects are created implicitly by specifying an `--out` directory when running an orchestrator. For example:
```bash
python3 -m artagents orchestrators run builtin.hype -- \
  --video source.mp4 \
  --brief brief.txt \
  --out /path/to/project_name \
  --render
```
The `--out` parameter **defines the project root** and is created on-demand (line 1019 in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/executors/cut/run.py`):
```python
out_dir = args.out.resolve()
out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
```

### 2. **Project Directory Layout**
At the project level (`args.out`), storage includes:
- **`pool.json`** — Shared asset pool of media clips/sources (per-source level)
- **`scenes.json`**, **`transcript.json`**, **`shots.json`**, **`quality_zones.json`** — Video analysis outputs
- **`briefs/`** — Subdirectory containing brief-specific working directories

Each brief creates a subdirectory at `<out>/briefs/<brief_slug>/`, where `brief_slug` defaults to the brief filename stem (line 369 in hype/run.py):
```python
args.brief_out = (args.out / "briefs" / args.brief_slug).resolve()
```

### 3. **Timeline Creation (Orchestrator → Executor Pattern)**
`timeline.json` is **not created by CLI; it's written by the `cut` executor** during the arrange → cut pipeline. Timeline creation is implicit—there's no separate "create timeline" step. The flow is:

1. **Arrange executor** writes `arrangement.json` to `<out>/briefs/<brief_slug>/arrangement.json`
2. **Cut executor** reads the arrangement and writes three files to the same brief directory:
   - `hype.timeline.json` (line 1021 in cut/run.py)
   - `hype.assets.json` (line 1022)
   - `hype.metadata.json` (line 1023)

### 4. **Broad Storage at Project Level**
Living next to timeline at `<out>/briefs/<brief_slug>/`:
- **`arrangement.json`** — Arrangement data from arrange step
- **`hype.timeline.json`** — Timeline config (created by cut executor)
- **`hype.assets.json`** — Asset registry with file paths/URLs
- **`hype.metadata.json`** — Pipeline metadata (version, timestamps)
- **`refine.json`** — Editor refinements (optional, post-cut)
- **`brief.txt`** — Copy of the brief text
- **`editor_review.json`**, **`validation.json`** — Optional review/validation outputs

### 5. **Key File Paths**

| File | Created By | Path |
|------|------------|------|
| Project root | CLI (implicit) | `$OUT_DIR` |
| Pool | pool_build executor | `$OUT_DIR/pool.json` |
| Arrangement | arrange executor | `$OUT_DIR/briefs/$BRIEF_SLUG/arrangement.json` |
| Timeline | **cut executor** | `$OUT_DIR/briefs/$BRIEF_SLUG/hype.timeline.json` |
| Assets | **cut executor** | `$OUT_DIR/briefs/$BRIEF_SLUG/hype.assets.json` |
| Metadata | **cut executor** | `$OUT_DIR/briefs/$BRIEF_SLUG/hype.metadata.json` |

assets_path_out = out_dir / "hype.assets.json"

> AGENT

Here's the picture:

## Project ≈ an `--out` directory

There's no `artagents project new`. A "project" is just whatever directory you pass as `--out` to an orchestrator run. The orchestrator creates it on demand (`out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)`). The project's "name" is the directory name you chose.

Typical entry point:
```
python3 -m artagents orchestrators run builtin.hype -- \
  --video source.mp4 --brief brief.txt --out /path/to/<project_name> --render
```

## Layout under that project root

```
<project_root>/
  pool.json              # source-level: media pool, transcript, scenes, shots
  scenes.json
  transcript.json
  shots.json
  quality_zones.json
  briefs/
    <brief_slug>/        # one per brief — slug defaults to brief filename stem
      brief.txt
      arrangement.json   # written by the arrange executor
      hype.timeline.json # ← THE TIMELINE, written by the cut executor
      hype.assets.json   # asset registry (paths/URLs/etag/sha)
      hype.metadata.json # pipeline metadata (version, generated_at, clip provenance)
      refine.json        # optional, post-cut editor refinements
      editor_review.json # optional
      validation.json    # optional
```

So a project has a *source* layer at the root (`pool.json` and friends — derived once from the input video) and a *brief* layer under `briefs/<brief_slug>/` where each timeline lives. You can have multiple briefs (multiple timelines) per project sharing the same pool.

## How the timeline gets written

You don't create the timeline directly — the **cut executor** writes `hype.timeline.json` from the upstream `arrangement.json` (artagents/executors/cut/run.py around line 1021):

```python
timeline_path_out = out_dir / "hype.timeline.json"
assets_path_out   = out_dir / "hype.assets.json"
metadata_path_out = out_dir / "hype.metadata.json"
save_timeline(config, timeline_path_out)
```

Pipeline order: `pool_build` → `arrange` → `cut` (writes timeline) → optional `refine` → `render`.

## Does it have a name?

The file itself is always `hype.timeline.json` (prefix is the orchestrator's output stem — `hype` for `builtin.hype`). The human-readable identity is the **brief slug** (the subdir name under `briefs/`). The schema doesn't carry a `name` field; provenance lives in `hype.metadata.json` and the `theme` slug inside the timeline.

> AGENT

I'd lean toward a **compose layer**: keep each run's `hype.timeline.json` exactly as it is today (immutable, reproducible per-run output) and add a project-level `master.timeline.json` at the project root that *references* runs and places them on tracks at given offsets — so a "run" produces clips, and a separate "place" step (or flag) appends those clips into the master. The main tradeoff vs just promoting one shared timeline to the project root and having every run mutate it: more indirection (you need a small materialize step to flatten master + run timelines into something renderable), but you keep per-run reproducibility, avoid concurrent-write conflicts, and can re-arrange / remove a run without re-cutting.

A lighter variant if you don't care about per-run reproducibility: skip the compose layer, hoist `timeline.json` + `assets.json` + `metadata.json` to the project root, and have the cut executor *append* clips (with new ids) and *merge* the asset registry instead of overwriting — simpler, but every run mutates the same file and you lose the ability to cleanly diff/redo one run.

**Core shape**
- `Project` (e.g. `EarthBinland`) is a first-class persistent thing on disk: `projects/earthbinland/{project.json, timeline.json, runs/}`.
- `Run` = one immutable orchestrator/executor invocation that lives under the project (`runs/<run_id>/`) and produces clips + assets. Reproducible, never mutated.
- `Timeline` is *the* canonical project object. It doesn't embed clips — it holds **placements** that reference run outputs by `(run_id, clip_id)`, plus the position/track/trim/effects/transitions you layered on top.
- `materialize(timeline) → TimelineConfig + AssetRegistry`: a thin resolve step that walks placements, pulls the referenced clip + asset from each run, applies the placement's overrides and "forces on top" (entrance/exit/transition/effects), and emits a normal flat `TimelineConfig` the existing renderer already understands. So yes — that's how it renders.

**Cherry-pick from a run** falls out for free: a placement only references the clip ids you actually chose, so you can pull 2 of the 12 clips from a run and ignore the rest.

Here's the execution plan, ordered by what plugs into what.

## The key architectural call

The existing code is wired around `--out` as a per-run output directory, and the timeline schema is shared with reigh-app via `@banodoco/timeline-schema`. The cleanest path is: **don't touch any of that.** The project layer sits *above* it.

- Executors keep accepting `--out` and keep writing the same files. We just point `--out` at `<project>/runs/<run_id>/`.
- The "project timeline" (`<project>/timeline.json`) is a **new file format** — placements that reference sources/runs — distinct from the run-level `hype.timeline.json`. ArtAgents-only, no schema change for reigh-app.
- A `materialize` step flattens the project timeline + referenced run/source data into a standard `TimelineConfig` + `AssetRegistry` → existing renderer eats it unchanged.

This means almost nothing in `executors/` or the renderer changes. The work is a new module + a thin wrapper around the orchestrator runner.

## What gets built (file by file)

### New

```
artagents/core/project/
  __init__.py
  paths.py             # PROJECTS_ROOT resolution, slug→path, run_id minting
  schema.py            # Project, Source, Run, Placement, ProjectTimeline TypedDicts + validators
  project.py           # Project class: load/create, list_sources, list_runs, timeline()
  source.py            # add_source(file), enrich(source_id) → fills duration/fps/sha/probe
  run.py               # register_run(out_dir, orchestrator, brief, source_ids) → writes run.json
  timeline.py          # ProjectTimeline.load/save, .place(), .remove(), .materialize()
  cli.py               # artagents project {new, list, show, add-source, place, materialize, render}
```

### Modified (small touchpoints)

- **`artagents/setup_cli.py`** — register `project` as a top-level command alongside `orchestrators`/`executors`/`elements`. (One subparser line.)
- **`artagents/core/orchestrator/runner.py`** (around line 32) — `OrchestratorRunRequest` gains `project: str | None` and `run_id: str | None`. When `project` is set, `out` is computed as `<projects_root>/<project>/runs/<run_id>/` and `run.json` is written on completion. `--out` continues to work for non-project ad-hoc runs.
- **`artagents/orchestrators/hype/run.py`** (line 107 area) — argparse adds `--project` as an alternative to `--out`. Resolves to a per-run dir under the project. No other logic changes; `args.out` is still set, just to a project-managed path.
- **`artagents/timeline.py`** — add `materialize_project_timeline(project_timeline, project) → (TimelineConfig, AssetRegistry)`. Reuses the existing `TimelineConfig` validator on the output side, so the materialized result is contract-compatible.

### Untouched

- `executors/cut/run.py`, `executors/arrange/run.py`, `executors/pool_build/run.py` — they keep writing `<out_dir>/{hype.timeline.json, arrangement.json, pool.json}`.
- `executors/render/run.py` — still loads a flat `Timeline` via `Timeline.load(...)`. We just hand it the materialized output.
- `packages/timeline-schema/` and `banodoco_timeline_schema` — no schema change.
- All current tests of cut/arrange/render/pool_build keep passing.

## The plug-in seams (3 of them)

1. **CLI seam** — `artagents project new earthbinland` creates the dir + `project.json`. `artagents project add-source <slug> <file>` writes `sources/<source_id>/source.json` with `{kind, file}` and (lazily, on first read by anything that needs it) probes duration/fps/sha into the same file.

2. **Orchestrator seam** — when invoked with `--project earthbinland`, the orchestrator runner mints `run_id = <YYYYMMDDTHHMMSS>_<orchestrator>_<short-hash>`, sets `args.out = <project>/runs/<run_id>/`, runs the orchestrator unchanged, then writes `run.json` capturing `{orchestrator, brief, source_ids, started_at, finished_at, status, out_files}`. The run's per-step outputs (`pool.json`, `arrangement.json`, `hype.timeline.json`, `hype.assets.json`, `hype.metadata.json`) all land inside the run dir without touching executor code.

3. **Timeline seam** — `ProjectTimeline.materialize(project)`:
   - Walks placements.
   - For `source: {kind: "source"}`: reads `sources/<id>/source.json`, emits a `media` clip pointing at the file with `from`/`to` from the placement.
   - For `source: {kind: "run"}`: opens `runs/<run_id>/hype.timeline.json` + `hype.assets.json`, finds `clip_id`, copies the clip + its asset entry, applies placement-level overrides (`at`, `track`, `effects`, `transition`, `entrance`, `exit`).
   - Merges/dedupes the asset registry.
   - Returns `(TimelineConfig, AssetRegistry)` → calls existing `validate_timeline` → `Timeline.load`-compatible JSON → renderer.

## Order of work (concrete)

I'd build it in 4 PR-sized chunks:

1. **Skeleton + CLI** — `core/project/{paths,schema,project,source,cli}.py`, `artagents project {new, list, show, add-source}`. Tests for slug validation, env-overridable root, source enrichment. ~400 LOC + tests.
2. **Project timeline + materialize (source-only)** — `ProjectTimeline` schema, `place`/`remove`, `materialize` resolving only `kind: "source"` placements. `artagents project materialize <slug>` writes a flat `TimelineConfig` next to the project. End-to-end: place 3 raw clips, materialize, hand to render executor — verify it plays.
3. **Run integration** — orchestrator runner gets `--project` + `run_id` minting + `run.json`. `materialize` learns `kind: "run"` placements. `artagents project place-run <slug> <run_id>` (interactive picker or all-clips default). End-to-end: run hype against a project, cherry-pick 2 of its clips, materialize, render.
4. **Tests + migration** — port `test_hype_cut_invariants.py` etc. to use a tmp project root via `ARTAGENTS_PROJECTS_ROOT`. Document the `--out` vs `--project` choice in the orchestrator help text. Don't deprecate `--out` — keep both.

## Two open decisions worth flagging

- **Sources at project root, or under `sources/<id>/media/`?** I'd keep media files where the user originally had them and just store a path in `source.json` — copying GBs into every project is wasteful. Add an opt-in `--copy` flag for `add-source` if isolation matters.
- **What does `place` look like as a CLI?** Lowest-friction: edit `timeline.json` by hand, with a JSON Schema for editor autocomplete. CLI `place` commands are nice but a long way down the priority list. Agents and a future UI can mutate the file directly.

> AGENT

Thinking from the agent's perspective — agents reason from text and have no implicit shell state across invocations. The UX has to make "where am I" *visible in the transcript*, not hidden in a session.

## The principle: explicit + echoed, no global state

Agents are bad at hidden state. Two rules cover most of it:

1. **`--project <slug>` is required for any state-changing command.** No `~/.artagents/state.json` "current project" file. Two agents working in parallel must never clobber each other.
2. **Every command echoes its scope as the first line of stdout.** Something like:
   ```
   project: earthbinland | timeline: main | root: ~/Documents/.../earthbinland
   ```
   That way the agent's context window always shows what just happened to *which* project. Same for runs — print the freshly minted `run_id` and its `out` path.

CWD-based inference is a nice convenience for humans but I'd skip it for agents — it makes the meaning of a command depend on where the previous tool call left them, which is exactly the kind of thing that breaks across long agent runs.

## Creating a project — the linear flow

```
artagents project new earthbinland --theme banodoco-default
artagents project add-source earthbinland ~/footage/cabin.mp4 \
  --description "wide shot, cabin at dusk"
artagents orchestrators run builtin.hype -- --project earthbinland --brief brief.txt
# prints: run_id=20260503T101500_hype_a1b2c3, out=<project>/runs/<run_id>/

artagents project place earthbinland --run <run_id> --clip c7 --at 4.2 --track v1
artagents project render earthbinland
```

Each step prints what it did and where. An agent reading the transcript a thousand tokens later can still answer "which project, which run, which timeline."

## "Which project am I in?" — `artagents project show`

The single discoverability command. Prints a tree:

```
earthbinland (theme: banodoco-default, created: 2026-05-01)
  sources (3): cabin-001 (video, 92.4s), riverbank-002 (image), wind-003 (audio)
  runs (2):
    20260503T101500_hype_a1b2c3 — brief: "trailer cut", clips: 12, status: ok
    20260503T143000_hype_d4e5f6 — brief: "shorter cut",  clips:  8, status: ok
  timelines (1):
    main — 7 placements, 2 runs referenced, total: 31.4s, valid ✓
```

Agents call this whenever they need orientation. `artagents project list` for the broader view across projects.

## Multiple timelines

Default is one (`<project>/timeline.json` = "main"). When extras exist, every command takes `--timeline <name>` and the echo header includes it. The "active timeline" concept doesn't need to live anywhere — `--timeline main` is the default everywhere, full stop. No `switch` command, no state.

## Errors as guidance

Agents recover from errors by reading them. Errors should always name the next command:

- `--project foo` doesn't exist → `unknown project 'foo'. existing: earthbinland, retro-99. create with: artagents project new foo`
- placement references missing run clip → `placement p_004: run '...' has no clip 'c99'. inspect with: artagents project show earthbinland --runs`
- `add-source` on a missing file → `file not found: .... did you mean ...?`

Plus an `artagents project doctor <slug>` that surfaces orphan runs, broken placements, missing source files, and stale enrichment in one shot. That's the agent's "health check" before render.

## What I'd defer

- **A `place` CLI with rich semantics** (split, ripple, snap-to-beat) — agents can edit `timeline.json` directly with the JSON Schema for autocomplete; build CLI surface only when patterns repeat.
- **Project templates / cloning** — nice-to-have, not load-bearing.
- **Multiple timeline names beyond "main"** — schema supports it from day one but no UX work until someone needs A/B variants.

Sound aligned? If so I'd lock this and start on chunk 1 (skeleton + `new`/`list`/`show`/`add-source`), since that's what every other piece needs to exist before it can be useful.

> AGENT

You're right — there isn't a general timeline-editing CLI. What exists:

- **`cut`** — *writes* `hype.timeline.json` from an `arrangement.json`. Not editing, just translation.
- **`refine`** — edits `arrangement.json` (dialogue trims) and re-runs cut. Operates one level upstream.
- **`inspect_cut`** — read-only viewer.
- **`open_in_reigh`** — hands the timeline off to the reigh-app web UI for visual editing.

So today the editing paths are: edit the *arrangement* and re-cut, edit the JSON by hand, or do it visually in reigh-app. There's no `artagents timeline place / move / delete / split` surface. That's a real gap for the agent UX you're describing — agents can't currently surgically modify a timeline from the CLI.

Two implications for the plan:

1. **For the project timeline (placements format), this is actually easier than editing `hype.timeline.json` would be.** A placement is small and structured (`{run_id, clip_id, at, track, effects}`), so a `project place / move / remove / list-placements` CLI is a few hundred lines, not a re-implementation of a NLE. Hand-editing the JSON is also genuinely viable because the schema is shallow.

2. **It explains why I'd defer rich editing semantics.** No prior surface to extend, so we don't owe backwards compat. Start with the four primitives (`place`, `move`, `remove`, `list`) and let real agent usage tell us what else (`split`, `ripple`, `snap-to-beat`, `replace-source`) earns its keep.

So: when I said "agents can edit `timeline.json` directly" earlier, that's true *because we're building a new placement-based timeline*. It would not be true for `hype.timeline.json` — that one's complex enough that you really do want the cut→arrangement→refine loop or the reigh-app UI.

Does that change anything about the order of work? I'd still start with the project skeleton + `new`/`list`/`show`/`add-source`, but add `project place` / `project list-placements` / `project remove` to chunk 2 (alongside materialize) so the editing surface lands the same time the placement schema does.

Honest answer: **plausible but tight, and it depends on what "done" means.**

If "done" = the four chunks I sketched, with chunk 1-3 solid and chunk 4 polished, one person full-time, no other context-switching → **yes, just barely.** Rough budget:

- **Chunk 1 (skeleton + CLI):** 2-3 days. Mostly mechanical.
- **Chunk 2 (placements + materialize source-only + place/move/remove):** 3-4 days. The schema design conversation is the real cost; the code is small.
- **Chunk 3 (run integration):** 3-4 days. The riskiest piece — touches the orchestrator runner's subprocess + thread wiring, asset path rewriting in `materialize` for `kind: "run"` references, run_id minting and `run.json` provenance.
- **Chunk 4 (tests, doctor, migration):** 2-3 days.

That's 10-14 working days. Two weeks = 10. So the realistic version of "done in a sprint" is **chunks 1-3 fully landed, chunk 4 partial** — meaning the model works end-to-end, but you'd ship with rougher error messages and fewer tests than ideal.

**What blows the budget if you're not careful:**
- Asset path normalization in `materialize` (run dirs vs project root vs absolute paths the renderer can resolve) — easy day-eater.
- Anything that requires schema changes to `@banodoco/timeline-schema` — coordinating across repos kills sprint velocity. The plan I sketched avoids this; protect that decision.
- Reigh-app integration (open the project timeline visually) — out of scope. If it's a must-have, plan for a second sprint.
- Treating the placement CLI as "rich" — keep it to four primitives and resist `split`/`ripple`/`snap-to-beat` requests until after the sprint.

**What I'd cut if it slips:**
1. `project doctor` (defer to sprint 2).
2. `--copy` mode for `add-source` (use file paths only).
3. Any "many timelines per project" UX beyond the schema being capable of it. Ship with `main` only.

**What I'd protect:**
1. The decoupling: project timeline is a *new* file format; existing executors and the shared schema stay untouched. This is the load-bearing architectural call.
2. End-to-end render path working from day-one of chunk 2, even if only with `kind: "source"` placements. Lets you smoke-test continuously.
