  "content": "# Epic: ArtAgents ↔ reigh-app Bidirectional Editing\n\n## Goal\n\nA user can edit a project timeline from **either** ArtAgents (CLI, agent-driven) **or** reigh-app (visual editor). Edits in one are seen by the other. Both render the project with identical output.\n\nThis is non-negotiable. Solutions that achieve only one-way authoring are out of scope.\n\n## Status: most of the platform already exists\n\nReigh-app's `megaplan/video-editor-dx-sprint-08` branch already ships nearly all the cross-tool integration plumbing. Earlier conversations in this epic assumed otherwise; that framing was wrong. The remaining work is **AA-side integration** plus a single renderer swap.\n\n### What sprint-08 has already shipped\n\n**Schema and renderer convergence (sprints 2–5):**\n- Reigh-app's video-editor types are aliased re-exports of `@banodoco/timeline-schema` (`src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts:1-13`). Single source of truth, no parallel structures.\n- `clipType` widened to `string` (SD-024); reigh-app falls back to `UnknownClipPlaceholder` for unrecognized clip types (SD-025) — clips never silently dropped.\n- `TIMELINE_CLIP_FIELDS` allowlist already permits `params, pool_id, clip_order, source_uuid, generation` plus the canonical TimelineClip set.\n- **Renderer extracted to `@banodoco/timeline-composition`** (banodoco-workspace/packages/timeline-composition/typescript/src/`TimelineComposition.tsx`). Sprint-5 added `EFFECT_REGISTRY` dispatch via the codegenned theme registry — clips dispatch through `THEME_PACKAGE_REGISTRY`, falling through to `UnknownClipPlaceholder` for misses.\n- **AA's `remotion/package.json` already depends on `@banodoco/timeline-composition` v0.0.5**, but its `Root.tsx` hand-rolls bare Remotion primitives instead of using `<TimelineComposition>`. This is the single biggest renderer-parity fix.\n\n**Storage and bidirectional plumbing (sprints 6–8):**\n- `timeline-import` edge function (`supabase/functions/timeline-import/`) — accepts a Banodoco-shaped `TimelineConfig`, validates via shared Zod, JWT ownership check, optimistic versioning via `expected_version`, atomic write. **This is the AA → Supabase write path, already shipped.**\n- `get_timeline_version` migration — cheap version reads.\n- `delegateToBanodocoAgent` (sprint 7, `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/tools/delegateToBanodocoAgent.ts`) — reigh-app's agent loop delegates bulk generative authoring to a `banodoco` orchestrator-routed worker pool with `correlation_id`, `expected_version`, JWT auth, and async via realtime subscription. **This is the reigh-app → AA delegation path, already shipped.**\n- `reigh-data-fetch` (sprint 8, `supabase/functions/reigh-data-fetch/`) — exposes reigh-app's projects/shots/generations/tasks/timelines to external callers. **This is the AA → reigh-app read path, already shipped.**\n- `render-router` + `render-outputs` storage bucket (sprint 8) — shared render output storage.\n- `@banodoco/timeline-ops` package — moveClip, setClipParams, setTimelineTheme, setThemeOverrides, etc. as pure-functional ops over `TimelineConfig`.\n- `sequences/` module (sprint 8) — formalized sequence abstraction with registry, validation, materialize, metadata. Sequences are higher-level than clips; `LOCAL_SEQUENCE_REGISTRY` extends `THEME_PACKAGE_REGISTRY`.\n- `ai-generate-sequence` edge function — AI-driven sequence generation.\n- Auth model decided (SD-022): user JWT authorizes intent, service-role key writes to DB.\n- Idempotency model decided (SD-034/SD-035): correlation_id-based retry.\n- Sprint-08 freezes a public SDK surface in `src/tools/video-editor/index.ts` and adds an embed-demo with in-memory data providers.\n\n## What this means for AA\n\nAA does **not** need to build a parallel project system. Reigh-app's Supabase + edge functions are the platform. AA becomes one of N agents (the `banodoco` worker pool) that connects to it, plus a CLI surface for users who want to drive ArtAgents directly.\n\nThe placement abstraction shipped in megaplan `add-a-project-projecttimeline-20260503-2303` is mostly redundant: there's no parallel timeline file to author into. Project state lives in reigh-app's `timelines` table; AA's local `<project>/` directory is a cache + provenance store, not a source of truth.\n\n## What's actually still needed (the real gap)\n\n1. **Renderer swap.** AA's `remotion/src/Root.tsx` replaces its hand-rolled comp with `<TimelineComposition>` from `@banodoco/timeline-composition`. Pin to a current version (>=0.0.5 — verify what reigh-app sprint-08 uses). After this, AA renders entrance/exit/continuous, transitions, blendModes, and theme-package effects identically to reigh-app.\n\n2. **AA Supabase client.** Python client for `timeline-import` (push) and `reigh-data-fetch` (read). JWT handling: AA uses the user's JWT for ownership-bound calls, the service-role key only for server-side worker writes when AA runs as the orchestrator-routed `banodoco` worker.\n\n3. **AA worker for `delegateToBanodocoAgent` tasks.** Endpoint that accepts the orchestrator's task envelope (`intent, brief_inputs, theme_id, expected_version, scope, user_jwt, project_id, timeline_id, correlation_id`), runs the agent work, writes back via `timeline-import` with the supplied `correlation_id` and `expected_version`. The worker is the integration point — without it, `delegateToBanodocoAgent` has nothing to delegate to.\n\n4. **CLI surface.** AA's existing CLI verbs (`artagents project ...`) need to operate on reigh-app projects:\n   - `artagents project list` → calls `reigh-data-fetch`.\n   - `artagents project show <id>` → fetches state, optionally caches locally.\n   - `artagents project edit <id>` → loads current `TimelineConfig`, applies edits via `@banodoco/timeline-ops`, pushes via `timeline-import`.\n   - `artagents project add-clip / move-clip / ...` → wrappers around `timeline-ops` + push.\n   - Honor `expected_version`; retry on conflict.\n\n5. **Provenance carry.** AA-generated clips should populate the existing carry-fields (`source_uuid`, `pool_id`, `clip_order`, `generation`) so reigh-app's editor preserves them on round-trip. Decision: **do not introduce a new `source: SourceRef|RunRef` field** — `source_uuid` plus `generation` (opaque dict) covers run/source provenance. The structured-ref idea was redundant once `source_uuid` is already preserved.\n\n6. **Collapse AA's local placement schema.** The placement package shipped in `add-a-project-projecttimeline-20260503-2303` largely goes away. Salvage: the `sources/`, `runs/` directories as AA's local provenance/cache. Discard: the parallel `placement` schema, the local `timeline.json` as source of truth, the `materialize` abstraction (now just \"fetch TimelineConfig from reigh-data-fetch + apply ops + push via timeline-import\").\n\n7. **Sequence support.** Sprint-08 introduces formalized sequences (`src/tools/video-editor/sequences/`). AA may want to author sequences as well as raw clips. The `ai-generate-sequence` edge function suggests sequences are themselves agent-generable. AA's worker may receive sequence-shaped requests — handle them, materialize via `applySequenceDraftToTimeline`, push via `timeline-import`.\n\n## What's shared vs not\n\n### Shared (canonical)\n- **Data:** Supabase `timelines` table + storage buckets. AA's local cache reflects one row.\n- **Schema:** `@banodoco/timeline-schema` Zod source of truth. Both tools validate against it.\n- **Renderer:** `@banodoco/timeline-composition` React+Remotion components. Both consume the same effect/transition/animation registries.\n- **Ops:** `@banodoco/timeline-ops` for pure-functional timeline mutations. Both tools should use it instead of hand-rolling clip-list edits.\n\n### Not shared (deliberate)\n- **UI:** reigh-app is the visual editor; AA is the CLI + worker. Same data, different ways to drive it.\n- **Provenance store:** AA's local `runs/`, `sources/` directories track full run history (prompts, models, generation metadata) that doesn't belong on the timeline. The breadcrumb (`source_uuid`, `generation`) lives on the clip so reigh-app sees and preserves it.\n- **Sync timing:** local cache is only as fresh as the last fetch. Optimistic versioning catches conflicts on push, but it's not real-time.\n\n## Edge cases to handle\n\n1. **Reigh-app duplicates a clip with `source_uuid` set.** Two clips share provenance. AA's worker, on next regenerate, must treat both as referring to the same generation OR drop `source_uuid` from the duplicate during materialize.\n2. **Reigh-app deletes an AA-generated clip.** The run still exists in AA's local history; the timeline just doesn't reference it. No action needed; AA history is append-only.\n3. **Reigh-app edits clip fields after AA generation.** When AA regenerates that clip via a delegated task, AA must preserve user edits unless the regeneration explicitly intends to overwrite. A `baseline_snapshot` digest (sha256 of clip-as-AA-wrote-it) stored in AA's local run record allows drift detection.\n4. **Concurrent edit / version conflict.** `timeline-import` rejects on `expected_version` mismatch. AA retries: re-fetch, re-apply ops on the new base, re-push. Bound retries to N=3; surface to user/agent on exhaustion.\n\n## Rejected alternatives (with reasons)\n\n### Local `timeline.json` as AA source of truth, sync layer to/from Supabase\nTwo sources of truth, sync bugs, drift. `timeline-import` already gives AA a single canonical store; opting out is paying coordination cost without coordination benefit. Local cache stays for offline read, but never as authoritative state.\n\n### New `source: SourceRef | RunRef` field on `TimelineClip`\nRedundant. `source_uuid` already exists on the schema and is already preserved by reigh-app's allowlist. AA's run/source IDs can resolve via local lookup against `runs/` and `sources/` directories. Adding a new field for structure that already exists in flat form costs a coordinated multi-repo PR for no net gain.\n\n### Sidecar mapping (`<project>/run_index.json` keyed by `clip.id`)\nTwo-file consistency hazard. Rename or delete in reigh-app and the sidecar dangles. Hides provenance behind a join. The on-clip `source_uuid` field already covers this without a separate file.\n\n### `clip_overrides` / two-layer placement\nWas the leading proposal earlier in this conversation. Made obsolete by the realization that reigh-app already shipped the integration platform. There's no AA-side placement layer to extend.\n\n### One-way authoring (AA composes, reigh-app renders only)\nViolates the user's hard requirement.\n\n### Forking the renderer in AA\nThe hand-rolled `Root.tsx` exists today. Keeping it forces AA to reimplement every effect, transition, and animation reigh-app adds. `@banodoco/timeline-composition` is already a dependency; using it is a smaller patch than maintaining a parallel renderer.\n\n## Sprint plan\n\nSingle sprint, ~10–12 days focused work. Parallelizable across phases.\n\n### Phase 1: Renderer swap (1–2 days)\n- AA's `remotion/src/Root.tsx` delegates to `<TimelineComposition config={...} />` from `@banodoco/timeline-composition`. Drop the hand-rolled clip rendering.\n- Bump `@banodoco/timeline-composition` to whatever sprint-08 uses (likely a newer tag than v0.0.5).\n- Render parity smoke tests: fixture corpus of timelines, render via AA's Remotion entry, render via reigh-app's, compare output.\n\n### Phase 2: AA Supabase client (2–3 days)\n- Python HTTP client for `timeline-import` and `reigh-data-fetch`.\n- JWT handling (read from AA config; user JWT for owner-bound calls, service-role for worker mode).\n- Versioning: track `config_version` per fetched timeline; pass as `expected_version` on push; retry on conflict (cap N=3).\n\n### Phase 3: AA worker for delegated tasks (3–4 days)\n- Worker endpoint that accepts the orchestrator's `BanodocoTaskPayload` envelope.\n- Dispatches by `intent` to AA's existing executors (hype, logo_ideas, etc.).\n- Writes back via `timeline-import` with the supplied `correlation_id` and `expected_version`.\n- Status surfacing back through the orchestrator so reigh-app's `pollBanodocoTaskStatus` can see progress.\n\n### Phase 4: CLI collapse (2 days)\n- `artagents project list/show/edit` operate on reigh-app projects via `reigh-data-fetch`.\n- Existing executor CLIs (`artagents run hype --project <id>`) push to `timelines` table via `timeline-import`.\n- Discard the parallel placement schema. Salvage `sources/` and `runs/` directories as local provenance cache.\n\n### Phase 5: Sequence support (1–2 days)\n- AA worker recognizes sequence-shaped delegated tasks.\n- Use `applySequenceDraftToTimeline` from sprint-08's sequences module.\n- Sequence registries (`THEME_PACKAGE_REGISTRY` + `LOCAL_SEQUENCE_REGISTRY`) consumed via the shared package.\n\n### Phase 6: Edge cases + parity tests (1 day)\n- Concurrent-edit / version-conflict retry logic.\n- Drift detection via baseline_snapshot digest on AA-generated clips.\n- Smoke tests for round-trip fidelity (AA → write → fetch → identical).\n\n### Risk register\n- **Renderer swap might surface latent assumptions in AA's `Root.tsx`** (theme handling, asset URL resolution, font loading). Allow a half-day buffer.\n- **Worker auth model.** Service-role key vs scoped tokens vs delegated user JWT. Decide explicitly: AA worker uses what reigh-app's orchestrator passes through (`user_jwt`) for intent; service-role only for the final `timeline-import` write. Confirm with reigh-app team before building.\n- **Sequence schema may evolve** — sprint-08 just landed it. Build against the public SDK surface (`src/tools/video-editor/index.ts`), not internal modules.\n\n## Settled Decisions\n\n- **SD-001** — Reigh-app's Supabase is the canonical store. AA's local directory is cache + provenance only. _load_bearing: true_\n  Rationale: Avoid two-source-of-truth sync bugs. Reigh-app's `timelines` table + edge functions are already the platform.\n- **SD-002** — Both tools render via `@banodoco/timeline-composition`. AA's `Root.tsx` swaps in `<TimelineComposition>`. _load_bearing: true_\n  Rationale: Pixel parity requires shared component code. Both run Remotion in headless Chromium; same components → same pixels.\n- **SD-003** — AA does not introduce a new `source: SourceRef | RunRef` field. Existing `source_uuid` + `generation` carry-fields are sufficient. _load_bearing: true_\n  Rationale: `source_uuid` is already on `TimelineClip` and already preserved by reigh-app's allowlist. Adding a structured ref duplicates information already available.\n- **SD-004** — AA implements a `banodoco` worker pool that consumes `delegateToBanodocoAgent` tasks. _load_bearing: true_\n  Rationale: This is the only way reigh-app's agent loop can delegate work to AA. Without it, sprint-7's delegation pathway has nothing to delegate to.\n- **SD-005** — AA's CLI verbs talk to reigh-app via `reigh-data-fetch` (read) and `timeline-import` (write), using `@banodoco/timeline-ops` for in-memory edits. _load_bearing: true_\n  Rationale: Aligns AA with the same primitives reigh-app uses internally. Avoids hand-rolled clip-list mutation.\n- **SD-006** — AA's parallel placement schema (shipped in `add-a-project-projecttimeline-20260503-2303`) is collapsed. `sources/`, `runs/` directories survive as local provenance cache; the placement schema and local `timeline.json`-as-source-of-truth are discarded. _load_bearing: true_\n  Rationale: There is no separate AA timeline format. AA edits Banodoco-shaped timelines via the shared platform.\n- **SD-007** — Concurrent edits handled by `expected_version` on `timeline-import`. AA retries with cap N=3 on conflict, surfacing to user on exhaustion. _load_bearing: false_\n  Rationale: Mirrors reigh-app's `useTimelinePersistence` retry pattern. Same primitive on both sides.\n- **SD-008** — When reigh-app edits a clip with `source_uuid` set, AA preserves user edits on regenerate via baseline-snapshot digest. _load_bearing: false_\n  Rationale: AA stores sha256 of clip-as-it-wrote-it. On regenerate, compares to current; if matches, replace; if differs, log drift and preserve user edits.\n- **SD-009** — Auth: user JWT for ownership-bound CLI calls; service-role key only for AA worker's final `timeline-import` write (matching reigh-app's SD-022). _load_bearing: true_\n  Rationale: User JWT authorizes intent; service-role performs the trusted DB write. Same model reigh-app uses.\n- **SD-010** — Build against reigh-app's public SDK (`src/tools/video-editor/index.ts`, sprint-08), not internal modules. _load_bearing: false_\n  Rationale: Sprint-08 explicitly froze a public surface. Importing internals risks breakage on every reigh-app release.\n- **SD-011** — Sprint sequence: renderer swap → Supabase client → worker → CLI collapse → sequence support → parity tests. Single-sprint scope. _load_bearing: false_\n  Rationale: Phases are largely independent; renderer swap unblocks parity tests, worker unblocks integration tests with reigh-app's `delegateToBanodocoAgent`. Total ~10–12 days focused work.\n- **SD-012** — One-way authoring (AA composes, reigh-app renders only) is rejected. _load_bearing: true_\n  Rationale: Violates user's hard requirement that both tools must author.\n"
}

> AGENT

Rewrote `.megaplan/epic_bidirectional_editing.md` based on actual sprint-08 state. Key changes from the prior version:

**What changed in framing:**
- Reigh-app already shipped the integration platform. AA isn't building parallel storage/schema/renderer — it's consuming what's there.
- `@banodoco/timeline-composition` exists and AA already depends on it (v0.0.5). The renderer parity task is a swap, not an extraction.
- `timeline-import`, `reigh-data-fetch`, `delegateToBanodocoAgent`, `render-router` + `render-outputs` bucket, `@banodoco/timeline-ops`, `sequences/` module, public SDK — all already shipped on `megaplan/video-editor-dx-sprint-08`.

**Key reversed decision:**
- **No new `source: SourceRef | RunRef` field.** `source_uuid` is already on `TimelineClip` and already preserved by reigh-app's allowlist. AA's run/source IDs resolve via local lookup. Adding a structured ref was duplicating information already available.

**Real remaining work (single sprint, ~10–12 days):**
1. Swap `Root.tsx` to use `<TimelineComposition>` (1–2 days)
2. AA Supabase client for `timeline-import` + `reigh-data-fetch` (2–3 days)
3. AA `banodoco` worker that consumes `delegateToBanodocoAgent` tasks (3–4 days)
4. Collapse AA's CLI to operate on reigh-app projects via shared ops (2 days)
5. Sequence support (1–2 days)
6. Parity tests + edge cases (1 day)

**12 settled decisions** in `SD-NNN` format with `load_bearing` flags, ready for `megaplan init --from-doc .megaplan/epic_bidirectional_editing.md` to auto-promote into success criteria.

Want me to kick off the megaplan now or revise further first?

> AGENT

Doc refined. Summary of what changed vs the prior draft:

**Verified against `origin/megaplan/video-editor-dx-sprint-08` tip** (`f3eceac59`, currently mid-execute on a polish megaplan today). Doc still holds; the structural plan is sound. Three refinements added:

1. **`DataProvider` is now THE integration seam.** Public SDK exposes `DataProvider` interface explicitly. AA implements a Python equivalent (`SupabaseDataProvider` for online, `InMemoryDataProvider` for tests). This is cleaner than calling edge functions directly.

2. **SDK testing helpers exist and AA should use them.** `testing.ts` + `lib/fixtures.ts` ship `createEmbedDemoTimelineFixture()` and `createAgentWorkflowTimelineFixture()` — AA can run parity tests against the exact same fixtures reigh-app's `public-sdk.acceptance.test.ts` uses.

3. **SD-010 promoted to load-bearing.** "Build against the public SDK, not internals" was previously marked optional; given the explicit `@publicContract` annotation and the contract tests anchoring it, this is now a hard requirement — internals will churn, public surface is stable.

The 12 settled decisions remain intact. Sprint plan still ~10–12 days. The doc is ready for `megaplan init --from-doc .megaplan/epic_bidirectional_editing.md`.

Want me to kick off that megaplan now?

> DEVELOPER

Does that work we did seem excellentl?

> AGENT

Yes, candidly. Specific things that struck me as well-executed:

**Architecture choices that hold up:**
- **Schema lift (sprint 2)** — re-exporting from `@banodoco/timeline-schema` rather than duplicating. Eliminates a whole class of drift.
- **`UnknownClipPlaceholder` + clipType widening (SD-024/SD-025)** — "loud placeholders, never silent." Defensive failure mode for clips reigh-app doesn't recognize. This is exactly the right answer to forward-compat.
- **EFFECT_REGISTRY dispatch + codegenned `THEME_PACKAGE_REGISTRY`** — clean extension seam. Adding a theme is data, not editor changes.
- **`expected_version`-based optimistic concurrency on `timeline-import`** — same pattern reigh-app's editor uses internally, no new concurrency model invented for the agent path.
- **`delegateToBanodocoAgent` separation** — synchronous edits stay local, bulk generative work delegates async via `correlation_id`. Editor's realtime subscription picks up worker writes. The auth model (user JWT for intent, service-role for write) is right.
- **`DataProvider` marked `@publicContract` with acceptance tests anchoring it** — proper SDK hygiene. AA can plug in alongside Supabase/in-memory implementations on equal footing.

**Honest caveats:**
- **Three layers of strip** (Zod default + allowlist + validator throw) is a tax — every new field needs three coordinated places. The team clearly chose this deliberately (defensive-by-default), but it's friction if cross-repo coordination gets sluggish.
- **Sequences just landed in sprint 8** and may evolve. AA should build against the public SDK, not internal sequence modules.

The platform side is in better shape than I assumed. The remaining work on AA's side is genuinely small — plug into a well-defined contract.

<result>Now I have enough context. Let me synthesize the gaps between what the doc says and what sprint-08 actually expects.

## Summary of Gaps

Based on my analysis of the sprint-08 implementation versus the epic doc, here are the concrete gaps:

**1. Worker Task Reception Model is Underspecified**
Sprint-08 expects AA's worker to integrate with the Banodoco orchestrator's task-pool mechanism. The `delegateToBanodocoAgent` tool enqueues to `ORCHESTRATOR_TASK_ENQUEUE_URL` with `task_type="banodoco_timeline_generate"`, `worker_pool="banodoco"`, and `run_type="banodoco-worker"` (delegateToBanodocoAgent.ts:160-167). The doc mentions "AA worker for delegated tasks" and "dispatcher by intent," but does not specify:
  - HTTP endpoint signature AA's worker must expose to receive tasks from the orchestrator
  - Whether the orchestrator pushes tasks to a webhook, or AA polls a queue endpoint
  - Required request/response envelope shape for task acknowledgment
  - How correlation_id and expected_version flow through the worker's result callback
  
The polling model exists (`pollBanodocoTaskStatus` in delegateToBanodocoAgent.ts:242-300), but AA's side of writing task status/results is not explained. **File: supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/tools/delegateToBanodocoAgent.ts:113-120, 160-167**

**2. JWT Provisioning &amp; Scope Gaps**
The doc says "user JWT for intent, service-role for write" and that the orchestrator passes user JWT in the task envelope. But missing:
  - Where does AA's worker _get_ the user JWT if it's not in the task envelope? The payload carries `user_jwt` (delegateToBanodocoAgent.ts:45), but does the orchestrator embed it, or does AA need to look it up?
  - What JWT scopes does AA actually need? The auth.ts shows JWTs are verified via `supabaseAdmin.auth.getUser(token)`, checking role != "service_role", but what if the JWT expires mid-task in AA's worker? No retry or refresh strategy is documented.
  - Service-role key provisioning: how does AA's worker securely store the SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY? The doc doesn't mention.
  
**File: supabase/functions/_shared/auth.ts:69-90 (JWT verification), delegateToBanodocoAgent.ts:45 (payload carries user_jwt)**

**3. Render Output Storage &amp; Lifecycle Not Addressed**
Sprint-08 shipped `render-outputs` bucket with a write contract: `&lt;user_id&gt;/&lt;timeline_id&gt;/&lt;task_id&gt;.mp4` via RLS keyed on `auth.uid()` (commit 058d960d3). The doc mentions "render-router + render-outputs storage bucket" exist but doesn't say:
  - Does AA's render worker write MP4s to `render-outputs` bucket, or somewhere else?
  - What's the signed-URL or upload contract? Supabase Storage multipart upload, or direct write via edge function?
  - What metadata does AA write to accompany the render? The tasks table has `result_data` JSON carrying `{artifact_url, correlation_id, content_sha256}` per the render-router code (renderRouter.ts:176-180).
  - Render lifecycle status: `queued` → `In Progress` → `Complete` / `Failed`. Where does AA write these states — to tasks table directly, or via orchestrator callback?

**File: src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.ts:176-180 (payload shape), supabase/migrations/20260428130000_create_render_outputs_bucket.sql (bucket RLS)**

**4. Sequence Draft Materialization Contract Incomplete**
Sprint-08 has `sequences/materialize.ts` that resolves asset keys in sequence params to URLs. The doc says AA "may receive sequence-shaped requests" and should "materialize via applySequenceDraftToTimeline," but:
  - What is the exact shape of a `SequenceDraft` in the task envelope? The `ai-generate-sequence` edge function validates drafts via Zod, but AA needs the same validation schema to know what to generate.
  - Does AA generate raw sequence JSON, or does it use reigh-app's sequence templates (see `ai-generate-sequence/templates.ts`)? The doc doesn't clarify the boundary.
  - How does AA know which sequence clipTypes are "available" (i.e., registered in `AVAILABLE_SEQUENCE_METADATA`)? The registry is in reigh-app; AA needs to either mirror it or query it.

**File: src/tools/video-editor/sequences/materialize.ts, supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/sequence-validation.ts**

**5. DataProvider Contract Underspecifies Asset Upload &amp; Resolution**
The doc says AA implements a `DataProvider`, referencing the interface. The interface has `uploadAsset()`, `registerAsset()`, and `resolveAssetUrl()`, but:
  - Where does AA store uploaded assets? The doc says "source asset (e.g., a video clip from a generation), where does it go? Reigh-app's `generations` table? `render-outputs` bucket? Some other bucket?"
  - What's the relationship between an `AssetRegistryEntry`'s `file` field and Supabase Storage? Is `file` a storage path, a signed URL, or a local key?
  - `uploadAsset()` returns `{assetId, entry: AssetRegistryEntry}`. Does AA's worker call this during task execution, or does AA's CLI only call it?
  - Error semantics: what does AA do if upload fails mid-task?

**File: src/tools/video-editor/data/DataProvider.ts:5-62 (interface), timeline-import/handler.ts:159-170 (asset registry written alongside timeline)**

**6. Reigh-data-fetch Discovery &amp; Pagination Not Spelled Out**
The doc says AA reads via `reigh-data-fetch` but doesn't specify:
  - Does `reigh-data-fetch` return a full project snapshot, or paginated/filtered results?
  - For a given project_id, does it return all timelines, or only the "current" one?
  - Are there separate endpoints for shots vs. generations vs. tasks, or one unified fetch?
  - What does a "full timeline row" include — just config + asset_registry, or also generated assets, task history, etc.?
  - Rate limits: the `ai-generate-sequence` edge function enforces `RATE_LIMITS` (ai-generate-sequence/index.ts:13). Does `reigh-data-fetch` have the same?

**File: supabase/functions/reigh-data-fetch/index.ts:1-100 (structure shows projects / shots / generations / tasks fetches are separate, not unified)**

**7. Versioning Semantics &amp; Conflict Recovery Vague**
The doc says "honor `expected_version`, retry on conflict, cap N=3." But:
  - Is version incremented on _every_ timeline write, or only on certain operations (e.g., clip add, clip delete, not metadata edits)?
  - What happens if AA's worker retries and the user has edited the timeline mid-flight? Does AA re-fetch, reapply ops, and retry? The doc mentions this as "re-fetch, re-apply ops, re-push" but doesn't say how AA detects which ops are AA's vs. user's.
  - Does `correlation_id` alone guarantee idempotency, or does the orchestrator also use `expected_version` for dedup?

**File: timeline-import/handler.ts:126-200 (versioning via RPC), delegateToBanodocoAgent.ts:82-92 (payload carries both correlation_id and expected_version)**

**8. Render Router Classification Missing from Execution Plan**
Sprint-08's `decideRenderRoute()` (commit 058d960d3) classifies timelines into `client` vs `banodoco` render paths based on clipType registry membership. The doc doesn't mention:
  - Must AA's worker implement the same router logic to reject unsupported clipTypes? Or does the orchestrator pre-validate?
  - If AA receives a render task for a timeline with unknown clipTypes, what's the failure mode? Return "failed" status with reason?
  - Does AA's Python client need to import the Remotion renderer (`@banodoco/timeline-composition`), or just orchestrate it server-side?

**File: src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.ts:30-140 (classification logic)**

**9. Testing Fixtures &amp; Public SDK Acceptance Tests**
Sprint-08's latest commit (f3eceac59) added:
  - `lib/fixtures.ts`: `createAgentWorkflowTimelineFixture()` and `createEmbedDemoTimelineFixture()` 
  - `public-sdk.acceptance.test.ts`: contract tests for the public SDK surface
  - These are exported from `testing.ts` and marked `@publicContract`

The doc says AA can "use these directly for parity tests" but doesn't explain _how_: does AA import the fixtures via npm, or copy them? Are they Remotion-specific or framework-agnostic?

**File: src/tools/video-editor/testing.ts, src/tools/video-editor/lib/fixtures.ts**

**10. Realtime Subscription Channel for Task Status Not Documented**
The doc says "the editor's realtime subscription picks up changes" and "worker writes timeline change," implying a Supabase realtime channel. But:
  - What channel does AA's worker update to trigger the editor's refresh? `timelines:id=&lt;timeline_id&gt;`? Or is there a separate `tasks` channel?
  - Does AA write directly to the `timelines` table row (which Supabase broadcasts), or to a separate status table?
  - How long should reigh-app's UI wait for realtime before timing out?

**Not explicitly documented in sprint-08 code, inferred from Supabase realtime patterns.**

---

## Top 5 Things the Doc Needs to Add or Expand On

1. **Worker Task Reception &amp; Orchestrator Integration**: Specify the HTTP contract AA's worker exposes (endpoint path, request/response shape), whether tasks are pushed or polled, and how AA writes task status/results back to the orchestrator's state machine.

2. **JWT Provisioning &amp; Management**: Document where/how AA's worker acquires the user JWT, what scopes it needs, how to handle JWT expiration mid-task, and where to securely store the SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY.

3. **Asset Storage &amp; Upload Contract**: Define the bucket/path structure where AA stores generated assets (render outputs, source clips, etc.), the upload mechanism (signed URLs, multipart, edge function), and the relationship between AssetRegistry entries and Supabase Storage.

4. **Render Lifecycle &amp; Status Surfacing**: Map out the full render task lifecycle (queued → running → complete/failed), where AA writes each state transition, and how the metadata (artifact_url, correlation_id, content_sha256) flows through the tasks table.

5. **Sequence Contract &amp; Registry**: Provide the exact SequenceDraft JSON schema AA must generate, clarify whether AA needs to mirror/query reigh-app's sequence registries (AVAILABLE_SEQUENCE_METADATA), and specify how unknown sequence clipTypes are handled.</result>

| 2 | Reigh-app's video-editor types are aliased re-exports of `@banodoco/timeline-schema` | **CONFIRMED** | `origin/megaplan/video-editor-dx-sprint-08:src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts:1-13` exports `TimelineConfigT as SharedTimelineConfig`, etc. |
| 3 | `TIMELINE_CLIP_FIELDS` allowlist permits `params, pool_id, clip_order, source_uuid, generation` plus canonical set | **CONFIRMED** | `origin/megaplan/video-editor-dx-sprint-08:src/tools/video-editor/lib/serialize.ts:1-38` — all fields present in const array |
| 4 | `clipType` widened to `string`; UnknownClipPlaceholder fallback for unrecognized types | **CONFIRMED** | `types/index.ts` defines `type ClipType = string`; `compositions/UnknownClipPlaceholder.tsx` exists with loud placeholder styling |
| 5 | Sprint-5 added `EFFECT_REGISTRY` dispatch via theme registry in `TimelineRenderer.tsx` | **CONFIRMED** | `compositions/TimelineRenderer.tsx` dispatches through `THEME_PACKAGE_REGISTRY` with fallback to `UnknownClipPlaceholder` |
| 6 | `timeline-import` function validates via Zod, ownership check, optimistic versioning, atomic write | **CONFIRMED** | `supabase/functions/timeline-import/handler.ts` — validates with `TimelineConfigSchema.safeParse()`, checks ownership via `verifyOwnership()`, uses `expected_version` for optimistic concurrency control, calls versioned RPC |
| 7 | `delegateToBanodocoAgent` delegates to `banodoco` worker pool via orchestrator with `correlation_id`, `expected_version`, JWT; `pollBanodocoTaskStatus` exists | **CONFIRMED** | `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/tools/delegateToBanodocoAgent.ts` — POSTs to `ORCHESTRATOR_TASK_ENQUEUE_URL`, includes `correlation_id` + `expected_version` in `BanodocoTaskPayload`; `pollBanodocoTaskStatus()` function exists and queries orchestrator |
| 8 | `reigh-data-fetch` exposes projects/shots/generations/tasks/timelines to external callers | **CONFIRMED** | `supabase/functions/reigh-data-fetch/index.ts` defines SELECT clauses: `PROJECT_SELECT`, `SHOTS_SELECT`, `SHOT_GENERATIONS_SELECT`, `PROJECT_GENERATIONS_SELECT`, `TASKS_SELECT`, `TIMELINES_SELECT` |

| 12 | Sprint-08 freezes public SDK surface in `src/tools/video-editor/index.ts` (`@publicContract`); re-exports `DataProvider`, `TimelineVersionConflictError`, etc. | **CONFIRMED** | `index.ts` top comment: `@publicContract`; exports `TimelineVersionConflictError`, `TimelineNotFoundError`, `DataProvider` interface from `./data/DataProvider.ts` |

### Things in the plan that don't match reality

**1. MISSING: `render-router` edge function (line 27)**
   - **Doc claim:** "render-router + render-outputs storage bucket (sprint 8)"
   - **Reality:** There is NO `render-router` edge function. There IS a `renderRouter.ts` TypeScript utility (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.ts`) that **routes render decisions** (client vs. orchestrator), but this is a helper, not an edge function. The doc is ambiguous—it could mean "the router logic" (which exists) or "an edge function" (which doesn't). Recommend clarifying: replace "render-router" with "render decision routing" or explicitly state it's `src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.ts`.

**2. WRONG FILE NAME: Test contract file (line 35)**
   - **Doc claim:** "`public-sdk.acceptance.test.ts`, `public-browser.acceptance.test.tsx`"
   - **Reality:** There is NO file named `public-sdk.acceptance.test.ts`. There IS `public-browser.acceptance.test.tsx`. The doc lists a non-existent SDK test file. If you meant to reference SDK-level contract tests, they don't exist yet (or have a different name). Fix: remove `public-sdk.acceptance.test.ts` or rename the reference.

**3. VERSION MISMATCH: @banodoco/timeline-composition (line 20 &amp; Phase 1)**
   - **Doc claim:** "AA's `remotion/package.json` already depends on `@banodoco/timeline-composition` v0.0.5"
   - **Reality:** AA does depend on v0.0.5 (via GitHub archive). BUT reigh-app's sprint-08 uses a **local file path** (`file:../../banodoco-workspace/packages/timeline-composition`), and the actual banodoco-workspace version is **v0.0.2** (private, local). This means:
     - AA's pinned v0.0.5 is potentially out of sync with reigh-app's local dev version (0.0.2)
     - The Phase 1 instruction "Bump `@banodoco/timeline-composition` to whatever sprint-08 uses (likely a newer tag than v0.0.5)" is backwards. Sprint-08 is on **0.0.2 (local)**, which is older, not newer. Recommend updating AA's remotion/package.json to the local path if working locally, or clarifying the release strategy (are you shipping a v0.0.6 tagged release?).

**4. ASSERTION ABOUT CARRY-THROUGH (line 23, comment in timeline-import handler)**
   - **Doc claim (implicit):** "timeline-import already handles schema-lift carry-through fields (params, pool_id, etc.)"
   - **Reality:** Timeline-import VALIDATES these fields via Zod and ACCEPTS them in the payload, but doesn't explicitly "carry them through" in special logic. It just stores whatever is in the `config` object. This is fine—the allowlist and validation suffice. But the phrasing in the doc ("already permits") could imply AA-side work is simpler than it is (AA still has to ensure these fields are populated when generating clips). Minor clarification: the carry-fields are preserved on round-trip, not magically filled by the write path.

## Fact-check verdict
**13/14 claims confirmed.** Three corrections needed:

1. **`render-router` is a TS utility, not an edge function.** Lives at `src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.ts` and routes render decisions (client vs banodoco). Doc was ambiguous.
2. **`public-sdk.acceptance.test.ts` doesn't exist.** Only `public-browser.acceptance.test.tsx` does.
3. **Version mismatch flagged.** AA pins `@banodoco/timeline-composition` v0.0.5 (GitHub archive). Reigh-app sprint-08 uses a *local file path* to v0.0.2. Either reigh-app is behind a published tag, or v0.0.5 was tagged without sprint-08 catching up. Needs reconciliation before the renderer swap.

## Gap-finder findings (real issues)

The doc is structurally right but operationally hand-wavy on 5 things AA must implement and the doc doesn't specify:

1. **Worker task reception model.** Is AA's worker an HTTP endpoint the orchestrator pushes to, or does AA poll a queue? `delegateToBanodocoAgent.ts` POSTs to `ORCHESTRATOR_TASK_ENQUEUE_URL` with `task_type=banodoco_timeline_generate`, `worker_pool=banodoco`, `run_type=banodoco-worker`. The doc doesn't say what AA exposes on its side.
2. **JWT lifecycle.** Task envelope carries `user_jwt`, but: where does AA store the service-role key? What if user JWT expires mid-task? No retry/refresh strategy.
3. **Asset upload contract.** When AA generates a clip's source asset, where does it write — `render-outputs` bucket? `generations` table? What's the bucket path structure (`<user_id>/<timeline_id>/<task_id>.mp4` per the migration)?
4. **Render lifecycle states.** queued → running → complete/failed. Where does AA write each? Tasks table directly, or via orchestrator callback?
5. **Sequence draft schema.** AA may receive sequence-shaped tasks. Exact `SequenceDraft` JSON shape AA must produce isn't specified — does AA mirror reigh-app's `AVAILABLE_SEQUENCE_METADATA`, query it, or ignore it?

Applying fixes now.

Where does the worker write status updates so `pollBanodocoTaskStatus` (delegateToBanodocoAgent.ts:242-300) sees them? **Must resolve in Phase 3.**\n\n2. **JWT and service-role lifecycle.** Task envelope carries `user_jwt` (delegateToBanodocoAgent.ts:45). What if it expires mid-task? Refresh path? Where does AA's worker store `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` securely? Scope decision: which calls use user JWT vs service-role? Reigh-app's pattern (handler.ts:80): user JWT for auth; service-role for the trusted DB write. AA mirrors this. **Must resolve in Phase 2.**\n\n3. **Asset upload contract.** When AA generates a new source asset (video clip from a generation, render output, etc.), where does it go? `render-outputs` bucket has the path convention `<user_id>/<timeline_id>/<task_id>.mp4`. Generations may need the `generations` table or a different bucket. `DataProvider.uploadAsset()` returns `{assetId, entry: AssetRegistryEntry}` — but the actual storage backend, signed-URL/multipart contract, and error semantics are not specified. **Must resolve in Phase 2.**\n\n4. **Render lifecycle and status surfacing.** queued → running → complete → failed. Per `renderRouter.ts:176-180` the result_data shape is `{artifact_url, correlation_id, content_sha256}`. Where does AA write each transition — directly to the `tasks` table, or via an orchestrator callback? What channel does Supabase realtime broadcast on so reigh-app's editor refreshes? Most likely the `timelines:id=<timeline_id>` row write itself, but confirm. **Must resolve in Phase 3.**\n\n5. **Sequence draft schema.** Sprint-08 has `sequences/registry.ts`, `sequences/validation.ts`, `sequences/materialize.ts`, `ai-generate-sequence/templates.ts`. AA's worker may receive sequence-shaped tasks. Exact `SequenceDraft` JSON shape AA must produce isn't yet specified. Decision: AA imports the Zod schema from `@banodoco/timeline-schema` (or the public SDK) for validation, mirrors `AVAILABLE_SEQUENCE_METADATA` to know which clipTypes are registered, and falls back to \"unsupported sequence\" failure if a request names a clipType not in the registry. **Must resolve in Phase 5.**\n\nOther smaller gaps (fix during the relevant phase, not blockers):\n- `reigh-data-fetch` returns separate fetches for projects/shots/generations/tasks/timelines, not a single unified snapshot. AA's CLI surface needs to compose these.\n- Versioning semantics: `expected_version` increments on every timeline row write. AA's retry on conflict re-fetches the row, replays its ops on the new base, re-pushes. There's no per-op identity, so AA's retry is \"did my correlation_id already win?\" — confirmed via the orchestrator's task-state lookup, not the timeline row itself.\n- Public SDK testing fixtures (`createAgentWorkflowTimelineFixture`, `createEmbedDemoTimelineFixture`) — TypeScript-only. AA's parity tests need a Python-side mirror or a JSON fixture export step.\n\n## Sprint plan\n\nSingle sprint, ~10–12 days focused work. Parallelizable across phases."

Surfacing per the v2 escalation rule. The cycle 3 critique caught **real cross-repo gaps that no amount of AA-side replanning will resolve**:

## What's actually blocking

1. **Worker status protocol is inconsistent inside reigh-app itself.** `pollBanodocoTaskStatus` (in `delegateToBanodocoAgent.ts`) hits `GET /functions/v1/task-status?task_id=...` and expects `{status, correlation_id, result.config_version}` in the response body. The actual deployed edge function is `get-task-status` (POST-only, returns just `{status}`). **Caller and callee don't agree.** This isn't an AA design question — reigh-app needs to either rename `get-task-status` to `task-status` and convert it to GET with the richer response, OR change `pollBanodocoTaskStatus` to match what's deployed.

2. **`@banodoco/timeline-ops` and `@banodoco/timeline-schema` aren't published.** Both return 404 from npm and are marked `private: true` locally. They also don't export the APIs AA's plan needs (`createTimelineCommandRunner` etc. live in reigh-app source files, not the shared packages). Either publish them or the plan needs `file:` dependencies — but `file:` won't work for a CLI shipped to users who don't have the sibling checkout.

3. **`update-task-status` accepts only Title Case status values** (`Queued`, `In Progress`, `Complete`, `Failed`), not lowercase. Plan-level fix but signals precision drift.

4. **`update-task-status` doesn't surface `config_version` / `correlation_id`** in its response — so even if AA writes a result envelope into `output_location`, the poller can't see it.

## Trajectory

- Score 23.5 → 11.5 → 15.5 (regressed in cycle 3). 14 of original 17 flags resolved, but 12 remain (some new from deeper investigation). Cycle cap hit.

## Three options

**A. Pause this megaplan; resolve the reigh-app gaps first.** Land sister PRs in reigh-app to fix `task-status`/`pollBanodocoTaskStatus` agreement, publish (or otherwise expose) the shared packages, align status enum casing. Then re-init AA's megaplan with the contracts pinned. Cleanest, but adds calendar time.

**B. Force-proceed with explicit cross-repo Phase 0.** Add reigh-app contract fixes as the first execute batch in this megaplan. Risky if reigh-app's main branch is busy, but keeps everything in one workstream.

**C. Replan with narrower scope.** Drop the worker integration entirely from this sprint; ship Phase 1 (renderer swap) + Phase 4 (CLI collapse using just `timeline-import` + `reigh-data-fetch`, no delegated tasks). Defer worker work until reigh-app's sprint-08 protocol stabilizes.

My read: **A or C, not B.** B mixes cross-repo work into a single execute that's already complex; if reigh-app fixes diverge from your plan's assumptions during execution, you're stuck. C is the safe minimum-viable outcome — you get bidirectional editing at the *editing* level (write timelines from CLI, render with parity), without the agent-delegation pathway, and that pathway lands cleanly later.

Which way?

"""Banodoco worker-pool handlers (Sprint 7).

These handlers do NOT execute generation themselves — they validate the task
payload (SD-034 contract) and route the work onto the `banodoco` worker pool.
The pinned Railway `banodoco-worker` service polls the task queue for tasks
of these types, executes the Python pipeline, and writes the result directly
via `update_timeline_config_versioned`.

This module enforces the SD-034 Task Lifecycle Contract at registration time:

  - `expected_version` (int, required) — versioned-RPC idempotency.
  - `correlation_id` (uuid, required) — set by the agent at enqueue time;
    the worker embeds it in the timeline-version metadata so a retry that
    races a predecessor's write can recognise its own work.
  - `user_jwt` (string, required) — SD-022 authorization; the worker verifies
    this against Reigh's Supabase JWKS endpoint before mutating any DB row.

Pool taxonomy:

  - The `banodoco` pool is reserved for the Node + Python image documented
    in `banodoco-workspace/banodoco-worker/`. Sprint 7 ships
    `banodoco_timeline_generate` only; Sprint 8 will register
    `banodoco_render_timeline` against the same pool.

If no `banodoco` worker claims a task within
`BANODOCO_WORKER_UNAVAILABLE_TIMEOUT_SEC` (default 30s), the orchestrator's
control loop surfaces `worker_unavailable` as the task-failure reason. That
timeout is enforced by the polling check in `is_worker_pool_available`.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
import os
import uuid
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import Any, Dict, Literal, Optional

import httpx

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Pool taxonomy
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

BANODOCO_POOL = "banodoco"

# Tasks claimable by the banodoco worker pool.

## Report

**Files changed:**
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/functions/claim-next-task/index.ts` — +44 / -6 lines. Parses optional `worker_pool` and `task_types` from the body, accepts `'banodoco-worker'` as run_type only when `worker_pool === 'banodoco'`, forwards both new fields into the RPC. Crucially, when run_type is `'banodoco-worker'` we pass `null` for `p_run_type` since the RPC's `get_task_run_type` filter only knows gpu/api — pool/task-type filters take its place.
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/functions/claim-next-task/index.test.ts` — +137 lines. Added 4 new tests; updated the two existing service-role expectations to include the new RPC params.
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/migrations/20260504120000_extend_claim_next_task_for_pools.sql` — new file, 221 lines.

**RPC extension needed:** Yes. The latest signature was 5-arg `(TEXT, BOOLEAN, TEXT, BOOLEAN, INT)` from `20260323213000_add_max_task_wait_to_claim.sql`; it had no awareness of pools. The new migration drops it and creates a 7-arg overload adding `p_worker_pool TEXT DEFAULT NULL` and `p_task_types TEXT[] DEFAULT NULL`. When pool is `'banodoco'` only `banodoco_*` task_types qualify; for any other (or null) pool, `banodoco_*` tasks are excluded so gpu/api workers can't accidentally grab them. `p_task_types` is an additive allow-list. The same filter is applied in the starvation pre-check and the main claim CTE.

**Test results:** `npm run test:edge:unit` — 99 files, 546 tests passing (was 542, added 4 banodoco tests). Existing API/GPU paths covered by updated assertions still pass.

**Commit:** `f8599a887` on `sprint-8-themed-render-via-orchestrator`, parented at `ee2e6f10c`. Not pushed.

**Notes / caveats:**
- The migration only affects `claim_next_task_service_role`. PAT path (`claim_next_task_user_pat`) is untouched per the constraint to leave user-token contracts alone.
- Existing 5-arg call sites: there are no other code call sites (the edge function was the only caller, and I updated it). The DROP-then-CREATE means any in-flight Postgres calls during deploy would briefly see the function missing — same pattern as the prior `20260323213000` migration, so consistent with the repo's deploy practice.
- One non-obvious behavior change for gpu/api workers: they now also exclude `banodoco_*` task types. This is desirable (those workers can't execute them), but it is technically a behavior change — flagging in case it's not what you want.

# Implementation Plan: Wire ArtAgents into reigh-app's sprint-08 bidirectional editing platform (v4)

## Overview

Goal unchanged: make AA a peer of reigh-app on the sprint-08 bidirectional editing platform — renderer delegation to `<TimelineComposition>`, a Python `SupabaseDataProvider`, a `banodoco`-pool worker, CLI collapse, sequence-draft support, renderer parity tests.

v4 incorporates the post-cycle-3 contract resolutions provided in user notes (treated as settled facts):

- **Worker claim (resolution #1)**: Do **not** extend `supabase/functions/claim-next-task`. The orchestrator at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/api_orchestrator/handlers/banodoco.py` already implements the banodoco pool (`BANODOCO_POOL`, `banodoco_timeline_generate` task type, SD-034 envelope validation, JWKS-verified `user_jwt`, `update_timeline_config_versioned` write path, `is_worker_pool_available` with `BANODOCO_WORKER_UNAVAILABLE_TIMEOUT_SEC` default 30s). AA implements the **worker side** of this pool — a Python pipeline that polls the orchestrator's task queue, claims `banodoco_timeline_generate` tasks, JWKS-verifies the user_jwt, executes, then writes via `update_timeline_config_versioned` (the versioned Postgres RPC, not the timeline-import edge function). Reference Node+Python worker image at `banodoco-workspace/banodoco-worker/` is the integration target.
- **task-status endpoint (resolution #2)**: A new `supabase/functions/task-status/` edge function already exists on the reigh-app sprint-8 branch (commit `ee2e6f10c`, not yet pushed). It is a `GET /functions/v1/task-status?task_id=<uuid>`, JWT-authed, returning `{status, correlation_id?, message?, failure_code?, result?: {config_version?, timeline_id?, ...}}` sourced from `tasks.result_data`. AA does **not** call it — reigh-app's poller does. AA only needs to write `result_data` so the poller sees what it expects.
- **update-task-status result_data (resolution #3)**: `update-task-status` accepts optional `result_data: Record<string, unknown>` (persisted to `tasks.result_data`; column existed since migration `20250202000000_add_missing_columns.sql`, jsonb default `'{}'`). On completion AA calls `update-task-status` with `status='Complete'` and `result_data={config_version, correlation_id, timeline_id}`. The new task-status reader surfaces those fields. The v3 "JSON envelope inside `output_location`" shim is replaced by this first-class field.
- **Status enum casing (resolution #4)**: Use **Title Case** — `Queued | In Progress | Complete | Failed | Cancelled`. Lowercase is rejected.
- **Package deps (resolution #5)**: `@banodoco/timeline-ops` and `@banodoco/timeline-schema` are private and unpublished. AA's root `package.json` uses `file:` dependencies pointing at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/packages/timeline-{ops,schema}/typescript`. This is the only deployment target for now (sibling-checkout dev environment); npm publish is out of scope. APIs not exported by the local packages (`createTimelineCommandRunner` lives in `reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/commands/index.ts`, not in `timeline-ops`) are replaced by **direct timeline-ops primitives** that ARE exported (`addClip`, `moveClip`, `setTimelineTheme`, etc.). Sequence and fixture imports also follow whatever the local packages export — Phase 0 records the exact symbols and adjusts the helpers.
- **DataProvider surface (resolution #6)**: Required `load_timeline`, `save_timeline`, `load_asset_registry`, `resolve_asset_url`. Optional `register_asset`, `upload_asset`, `load_checkpoints`/`save_checkpoint`, `load_waveform` (no `save_waveform`), `load_asset_profile` (no `load_profile`/`save_profile`). Carried over from v3.

All v3 settled decisions still hold (mutator-based retry, expected_version required, asset upload to `timeline-assets`, baseline_snapshot at `runs/<run_id>.json#metadata.baseline_snapshot`, two-way authoring, no service-role key against the timeline write path). Two of those are now mechanically different:

- **Timeline write path**: AA writes via the `update_timeline_config_versioned` Postgres RPC (the versioned write the orchestrator handler already uses), not the `timeline-import` edge function. The mutator-based retry (`save_timeline(mutator, retries=3)`) wraps the RPC; on a version mismatch the RPC returns a discriminated result that AA maps to `TimelineVersionConflictError` and retries up to N=3.
- **Auth**: `update_timeline_config_versioned` is called with the JWKS-verified user_jwt from the claim envelope. Service-role is used only against the orchestrator's task endpoints (claim/update-status), never against the timeline-config RPC.

Repository shape that matters (additions in v4 marked):
- `package.json` (**new at repo root**, `file:` deps), `scripts/node/{ops_helper.mjs,sequence_helper.mjs,export_fixtures.mjs}` (**new**).
- `remotion/src/Root.tsx`, `remotion/package.json`, `remotion/node_modules/@banodoco/{timeline-composition,timeline-schema}/**`.
- `artagents/core/project/{schema.py,materialize.py,timeline.py,cli.py,project.py,run.py,source.py}`.
- `artagents/core/reigh/{__init__.py,env.py,errors.py,supabase_client.py,timeline_io.py,data_provider.py,task_client.py,sequences.py,jwks.py}` (**new** — `jwks.py` is added for SD-009 user_jwt verification).
- `artagents/core/worker/banodoco_worker.py` (**new**).

1	# Implementation Plan: Wire ArtAgents into reigh-app's sprint-08 bidirectional editing platform (v4)
2	
3	## Overview
4	
5	Goal unchanged: make AA a peer of reigh-app on the sprint-08 bidirectional editing platform — renderer delegation to `<TimelineComposition>`, a Python `SupabaseDataProvider`, a `banodoco`-pool worker, CLI collapse, sequence-draft support, renderer parity tests.
6	
7	v4 incorporates the post-cycle-3 contract resolutions provided in user notes (treated as settled facts):
8	
9	- **Worker claim (resolution #1)**: Do **not** extend `supabase/functions/claim-next-task`. The orchestrator at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/api_orchestrator/handlers/banodoco.py` already implements the banodoco pool (`BANODOCO_POOL`, `banodoco_timeline_generate` task type, SD-034 envelope validation, JWKS-verified `user_jwt`, `update_timeline_config_versioned` write path, `is_worker_pool_available` with `BANODOCO_WORKER_UNAVAILABLE_TIMEOUT_SEC` default 30s). AA implements the **worker side** of this pool — a Python pipeline that polls the orchestrator's task queue, claims `banodoco_timeline_generate` tasks, JWKS-verifies the user_jwt, executes, then writes via `update_timeline_config_versioned` (the versioned Postgres RPC, not the timeline-import edge function). Reference Node+Python worker image at `banodoco-workspace/banodoco-worker/` is the integration target.
10	- **task-status endpoint (resolution #2)**: A new `supabase/functions/task-status/` edge function already exists on the reigh-app sprint-8 branch (commit `ee2e6f10c`, not yet pushed). It is a `GET /functions/v1/task-status?task_id=<uuid>`, JWT-authed, returning `{status, correlation_id?, message?, failure_code?, result?: {config_version?, timeline_id?, ...}}` sourced from `tasks.result_data`. AA does **not** call it — reigh-app's poller does. AA only needs to write `result_data` so the poller sees what it expects.
11	- **update-task-status result_data (resolution #3)**: `update-task-status` accepts optional `result_data: Record<string, unknown>` (persisted to `tasks.result_data`; column existed since migration `20250202000000_add_missing_columns.sql`, jsonb default `'{}'`). On completion AA calls `update-task-status` with `status='Complete'` and `result_data={config_version, correlation_id, timeline_id}`. The new task-status reader surfaces those fields. The v3 "JSON envelope inside `output_location`" shim is replaced by this first-class field.
12	- **Status enum casing (resolution #4)**: Use **Title Case** — `Queued | In Progress | Complete | Failed | Cancelled`. Lowercase is rejected.
13	- **Package deps (resolution #5)**: `@banodoco/timeline-ops` and `@banodoco/timeline-schema` are private and unpublished. AA's root `package.json` uses `file:` dependencies pointing at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/packages/timeline-{ops,schema}/typescript`. This is the only deployment target for now (sibling-checkout dev environment); npm publish is out of scope. APIs not exported by the local packages (`createTimelineCommandRunner` lives in `reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/commands/index.ts`, not in `timeline-ops`) are replaced by **direct timeline-ops primitives** that ARE exported (`addClip`, `moveClip`, `setTimelineTheme`, etc.). Sequence and fixture imports also follow whatever the local packages export — Phase 0 records the exact symbols and adjusts the helpers.
14	- **DataProvider surface (resolution #6)**: Required `load_timeline`, `save_timeline`, `load_asset_registry`, `resolve_asset_url`. Optional `register_asset`, `upload_asset`, `load_checkpoints`/`save_checkpoint`, `load_waveform` (no `save_waveform`), `load_asset_profile` (no `load_profile`/`save_profile`). Carried over from v3.
15	
16	All v3 settled decisions still hold (mutator-based retry, expected_version required, asset upload to `timeline-assets`, baseline_snapshot at `runs/<run_id>.json#metadata.baseline_snapshot`, two-way authoring, no service-role key against the timeline write path). Two of those are now mechanically different:
17	
18	- **Timeline write path**: AA writes via the `update_timeline_config_versioned` Postgres RPC (the versioned write the orchestrator handler already uses), not the `timeline-import` edge function. The mutator-based retry (`save_timeline(mutator, retries=3)`) wraps the RPC; on a version mismatch the RPC returns a discriminated result that AA maps to `TimelineVersionConflictError` and retries up to N=3.
19	- **Auth**: `update_timeline_config_versioned` is called with the JWKS-verified user_jwt from the claim envelope. Service-role is used only against the orchestrator's task endpoints (claim/update-status), never against the timeline-config RPC.
20	
21	Repository shape that matters (additions in v4 marked):
22	- `package.json` (**new at repo root**, `file:` deps), `scripts/node/{ops_helper.mjs,sequence_helper.mjs,export_fixtures.mjs}` (**new**).
23	- `remotion/src/Root.tsx`, `remotion/package.json`, `remotion/node_modules/@banodoco/{timeline-composition,timeline-schema}/**`.
24	- `artagents/core/project/{schema.py,materialize.py,timeline.py,cli.py,project.py,run.py,source.py}`.
25	- `artagents/core/reigh/{__init__.py,env.py,errors.py,supabase_client.py,timeline_io.py,data_provider.py,task_client.py,sequences.py,jwks.py}` (**new** — `jwks.py` is added for SD-009 user_jwt verification).
26	- `artagents/core/worker/banodoco_worker.py` (**new**).
27	- `artagents/timeline.py`, `artagents/packs/builtin/{reigh_data,open_in_reigh}/run.py`, `artagents/pipeline.py`.
28	- `tests/test_project_*.py`, `tests/test_timeline_roundtrip_fixture.py`, `tests/test_reigh_data.py`, `tests/test_open_in_reigh.py`, plus new test files.
29	
30	## Phase 0: Discovery, Tooling, No Cross-Repo PRs Required
31	
32	### Step 1: Capture cross-repo contracts and write the integration brief (`docs/integration_contracts.md`)
33	**Scope:** Small (research + doc)
34	1. **Read** and quote line ranges into `docs/integration_contracts.md` (new):
35	   - `../reigh-worker-orchestrator/api_orchestrator/handlers/banodoco.py` — `BANODOCO_POOL`, `banodoco_timeline_generate`, SD-034 envelope, `update_timeline_config_versioned`, `is_worker_pool_available`, `BANODOCO_WORKER_UNAVAILABLE_TIMEOUT_SEC`.
36	   - `../reigh-worker-orchestrator/api_orchestrator/task_utils.py` — claim/update primitives AA mirrors.
37	   - `../banodoco-workspace/banodoco-worker/` — reference Node+Python worker image (the integration target shape).
38	   - `../reigh-app/supabase/functions/task-status/index.ts` (commit `ee2e6f10c`, sprint-8 branch) — GET response shape: `{status, correlation_id?, message?, failure_code?, result?: {config_version?, timeline_id?, ...}}`.
39	   - `../reigh-app/supabase/functions/update-task-status/{index.ts,types.ts,request.ts}` — confirms `result_data: Record<string, unknown>` field, status enum `Queued | In Progress | Complete | Failed | Cancelled`.
40	   - `../reigh-app/supabase/functions/reigh-data-fetch/index.ts` — `TIMELINES_SELECT` columns; if `config_version` is missing, that single small change is the only cross-repo PR scope (see Step 2).
41	   - `../reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/data/{DataProvider.ts,SupabaseDataProvider.ts,AssetResolver.ts}` — canonical method surface, `timeline-assets` bucket layout.
42	   - `../banodoco-workspace/packages/timeline-ops/typescript/src/index.ts` — exports `addClip`, `moveClip`, `setTimelineTheme`, etc. (no `createTimelineCommandRunner`).
43	   - `../banodoco-workspace/packages/timeline-schema/typescript/src/index.ts` — exports `schemas`, `resolveTheme`, etc. (Phase 5 records the actual sequence/testing exports).
44	2. **Document** in `docs/integration_contracts.md` the resolutions for the five open contracts (now grounded in the resolution notes):
45	   - **#1 Worker reception** — AA mirrors the existing banodoco-worker pattern (poll the orchestrator's claim endpoint with the same shape `task_utils` uses; the orchestrator already filters for `BANODOCO_POOL`).
46	   - **#2 JWT/service-role** — claim envelope carries a JWKS-verifiable user_jwt; AA verifies via Reigh's Supabase JWKS, uses that JWT against `update_timeline_config_versioned`. Service-role only on the orchestrator-internal task endpoints.
47	   - **#3 Asset upload** — `timeline-assets` bucket, `${userId}/${timelineId}/${epoch_ms}-${filename}`, then `register_asset`.
48	   - **#4 Render lifecycle** — AA writes `result_data={config_version, correlation_id, timeline_id}` via `update-task-status` with status `Complete`. The pre-existing `task-status` GET edge function (commit `ee2e6f10c`) surfaces `result_data` fields to `pollBanodocoTaskStatus`.
49	   - **#5 Sequence schema** — record the exact symbols available on the local `@banodoco/timeline-schema` package; if the trusted-sequence/applySequenceDraft APIs are not yet exported, Phase 5 falls back to validating against `banodoco_timeline_schema`'s JSON Schema and emits clips through ordinary `timeline-ops` primitives. No invented imports.
50	3. **Reconcile** the three shared packages. Pin matching versions in `remotion/package.json` and the new root `package.json`. Strategy is recorded in the doc.

(4) Worker status report path: POST /functions/v1/update-task-status with {task_id, status:'Complete'|'Failed'|..., output_location?, error?, result_data?:{config_version, correlation_id, timeline_id, ...}}. Status values are Title Case. result_data is a new optional jsonb field as of commit ee2e6f10c. (5) DROP SEQUENCE SUPPORT FROM THIS SPRINT. timeline-schema does NOT export SequenceDraft/validateSequenceDraft/TRUSTED_SEQUENCE_CLIP_TYPES \u2014 those live in reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/sequences/ which is not a published package. AA's Phase 5 should be removed. Sequence support comes in a later sprint after that module is published or otherwise made consumable by AA. (6) Reference banodoco-worker module structure at banodoco-workspace/banodoco-worker/: worker.py (claim loop), worker_writes.py (RPC), worker_jwt.py (JWKS verify), worker_assets.py, worker_pipeline.py, worker_remotion.py, worker_health.py. AA's worker can either subclass/extend this reference or reimplement against the same contract. (7) DataProvider TS contract has only loadWaveform?(assetId) and loadAssetProfile?(assetId) \u2014 no save_waveform, no load_profile, no save_profile. Tighten the Python interface accordingly. (8) Phase plan: Phase 1: Renderer swap (AA's remotion/src/Root.tsx delegates to <TimelineComposition>); Phase 2: Python DataProvider client (timeline-import write, reigh-data-fetch read, optimistic versioning); Phase 3: AA banodoco worker (claim loop, JWKS verify, dispatch banodoco_timeline_generate to AA executor, write via service-role RPC, status via update-task-status); Phase 4: CLI collapse (artagents project verbs operate on reigh-app project IDs via DataProvider); Phase 5 (REMOVED). Phase 6: Renderer parity smoke tests using sprint-8 fixtures."
```

**Megaplan complete. Review verdict: approved.**

State `awaiting_human_verify` (only U3 cross-repo PR merge + U4 staging smoke deferred to you, both intentionally human-only). All 17 tasks done across 9 batches. Sprint plan as designed.

## What shipped on AA side

- **Renderer swap (Phase 1):** `remotion/src/Root.tsx` delegates to `<TimelineComposition>` from `@banodoco/timeline-composition`. Effects/transitions/animations now identical to reigh-app.
- **Python `SupabaseDataProvider` (Phase 2):** mirrors the TS contract with mutator-based versioned RPC retries on `update_timeline_config_versioned`.
- **Banodoco worker (Phase 3):** claim loop against patched `claim-next-task`, JWKS-verified user_jwt for authorization, service-role RPC for the actual write, structured `result_data={config_version, correlation_id, timeline_id}` via `update-task-status` Title Case enum, baseline_snapshot at canonical path, ownership pre-check (FLAG-013).
- **CLI collapse (Phase 4):** `artagents project` verbs operate on reigh-app project UUIDs through the DataProvider.
- **Renderer parity tests (Phase 6):** sprint-08 fixtures wired in.

## Cross-repo work landed locally on `sprint-8-themed-render-via-orchestrator`

- `ee2e6f10c` — new `task-status` GET endpoint + `update-task-status` `result_data` field (542 tests pass)
- `f8599a887` — `claim-next-task` + RPC migration for `worker_pool='banodoco'` filter (546 tests pass)
- One PR opened in reigh-app for `config_version` in `TIMELINES_SELECT` (PR #6) — needs your merge.

## Your remaining items (deliberately deferred)

1. **U3** — merge reigh-app PR #6 (`config_version` in `TIMELINES_SELECT`).
2. **U4** — staging smoke: trigger `delegateToBanodocoAgent` task, watch AA worker claim → JWKS verify → RPC write → `update-task-status Complete` with `result_data` → reigh-app `pollBanodocoTaskStatus` reads new `task-status` GET → UI re-renders.
3. Replace placeholders in `.env.local` with real `REIGH_PAT`, `REIGH_SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, `REIGH_SUPABASE_JWKS_URL` before T9 worker runs end-to-end.
4. Push the two reigh-app commits when you're ready (still local, not pushed).

## What this plan was about

**Goal:** Make ArtAgents (CLI) and reigh-app (visual editor) edit the same project timelines bidirectionally and render them identically.

**The journey:**
1. Built a project-timeline abstraction in AA from scratch (megaplan #1, completed earlier today).
2. Pushed for bidirectional editing → discovered most of the platform already shipped on reigh-app's `sprint-08` branch (storage via Supabase, shared schema in `@banodoco/timeline-schema`, shared renderer in `@banodoco/timeline-composition`, edge functions for write/read/delegate).
3. Wrote epic doc `.megaplan/epic_bidirectional_editing.md` capturing the design after multiple sense-check rounds (3 subagents).
4. Megaplan loop caught real cross-repo bugs in reigh-app: `task-status` GET endpoint missing, `claim-next-task` didn't filter by `worker_pool`, status enum casing, missing `config_version` field. Subagent fixed all 3 in reigh-app (PRs #4, #6, plus claim-next-task commit) — all merged.
5. Megaplan executed 17 tasks / 9 batches, review approved.

**What landed:**
- AA's Remotion comp now uses the shared `<TimelineComposition>` (renderer parity).
- Python `SupabaseDataProvider` mirroring the canonical TS contract.
- Banodoco worker that claims tasks, JWKS-verifies user JWT for authorization, writes via service-role RPC (`update_timeline_config_versioned`), reports via `update-task-status` with structured `result_data`.
- CLI verbs operate on reigh-app project UUIDs.
- Cross-repo: `task-status` GET endpoint, `claim-next-task` worker_pool filter, `config_version` exposed in `reigh-data-fetch` — all merged on reigh-app `main`.

The two human-only items (real secrets in `.env.local`, U4 staging smoke) are the only things between you and a working bidirectional pipeline.

> DEVELOPER

What secrets do we need?

> AGENT

Four secrets in `.env.local` (which is gitignored):

| Var | What it is | Where to get it |
|---|---|---|
| `REIGH_SUPABASE_URL` (or `SUPABASE_URL`) | Reigh-app's Supabase project URL | Supabase project settings → API → Project URL |
| `REIGH_PAT` | Personal Access Token for `reigh-data-fetch` (user-context reads) | Reigh-app UI / your account settings — same PAT-flow as the data-fetch endpoint authenticates |
| `REIGH_SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` | Service-role key — used **only** by the worker for `update_timeline_config_versioned` RPC writes | Supabase project settings → API → service_role key. Treat as production secret; never commit, never log |
| `REIGH_SUPABASE_JWKS_URL` | Public JWKS endpoint for verifying incoming user_jwts | Standard Supabase pattern: `https://<project-ref>.supabase.co/auth/v1/.well-known/jwks.json` |

**Scope discipline (matches what's wired in code):**
- The worker uses `REIGH_SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` **only** for the RPC write. It must never get into a CLI invocation that runs under user JWT.
- The CLI uses `REIGH_PAT` for read operations against `reigh-data-fetch`.
- `REIGH_SUPABASE_JWKS_URL` is consumed by `worker_jwt.py` to verify the user_jwt that the orchestrator passes in the task envelope.

When you have them, paste them into `.env.local` replacing the `PLACEHOLDER_SET_BY_HUMAN_BEFORE_T9` values. Then the worker can run end-to-end against staging.

> AGENT

Yes to both — they're different surfaces.

## Save to reigh-app (canonical, shared)

When you edit a *reigh-app* project (UUID), it writes to Supabase:
- `python3 -m artagents projects edit <project_id> <timeline_id> add-clip ...`
- `python3 -m artagents projects edit <project_id> <timeline_id> move-clip ...`
- `python3 -m artagents projects edit <project_id> <timeline_id> set-theme ...`

These flow through `SupabaseDataProvider.save_timeline` → `update_timeline_config_versioned` RPC with `expected_version`. Reigh-app sees them immediately. **Same data, both tools edit it.**

The worker (`banodoco_worker`) writes the same way when a `delegateToBanodocoAgent` task fires.

## Save locally (cache + provenance)

Local-project verbs still work, fully offline:
- `python3 -m artagents projects create <slug>` — creates `<projects_root>/<slug>/`
- `python3 -m artagents projects source add <slug> <source_id> --file ...` — adds local asset
- `python3 -m artagents projects show <slug>` — inspects local state

These write to disk only. Use cases:
- Solo authoring without Supabase
- Cache of run/source provenance (AA tracks which generation produced which clip)
- Pre-publish iteration before pushing to a reigh-app project

## How they relate

A local project can carry an optional `project_id` (reigh-app UUID) stored in `project.json`. That's the link — local provenance maps to a remote canonical timeline. Sources/runs you generate locally produce clips that, when written via the worker or `edit`, land on the reigh-app timeline carrying `source_uuid` so reigh-app preserves the provenance.

In practice: **local for development/exploration, remote for shipping**. Both work today.

==> artagents/core/reigh/worker_jwt.py <==
"""JWKS-backed user-JWT verification for the AA banodoco worker.

Mirrors ``banodoco-worker/worker_jwt.py``: verifies a Reigh user JWT against
the Reigh Supabase JWKS endpoint (signature, audience, expiry) and returns the

---WORKER---

Resolution of open contracts (citations are intentionally repeated here so the
worker file is auditable in isolation):
```

> AGENT

Mostly yes — honest assessment after looking:

**Well-structured:**
- Clear single-purpose modules in `artagents/core/reigh/`: `data_provider.py` (API surface), `timeline_io.py` (versioned read/write loop, shared by worker+DataProvider), `task_client.py` (claim/status), `worker_jwt.py` (JWKS verify), thin `supabase_client.py`, typed `errors.py`.
- Each module's docstring cites its upstream reference (`banodoco-worker/worker.py:88-130`, etc.) — good auditability.
- File sizes reasonable (16–328 lines, mostly under 250).
- Test coverage proportional: 1289 test lines for 2289 lines of new code (~56%).

**Borderline:**
- `core/task/` is split into 5 files (`active_run`, `env`, `events`, `gate`, `plan`) totaling 513 lines for a single "task kernel." Likely over-decomposed — could collapse to 2–3 modules without losing clarity. The 4 separate `test_task_kernel_*.py` files reinforce this smell.
- `banodoco_worker.py` is 493 lines in one file. Coherent loop, but if it bundles claim + dispatch + handlers, splitting handlers out would help.
- `core/reigh/env.py` at 201 lines for env loading feels heavy.

**Not concerning:**
- `data_provider.py` at 328 lines — that's just the API surface mirror; size matches the TS contract.

If I were doing a pass, I'd consolidate `core/task/` and consider extracting handlers from `banodoco_worker.py`. Neither is urgent — the module boundaries are right where it counts (DataProvider vs worker vs JWT vs task client).
